# SuperPaymaster - Agent Guidelines

## Project Overview

SuperPaymaster is a **decentralized gas payment infrastructure** for ERC-4337 Account Abstraction, built with Foundry (Solidity 0.8.33). It enables communities to sponsor gas fees for users via community tokens (xPNTs) instead of ETH.

### Two Operating Modes

- **AOA+ Mode** (SuperPaymaster): Shared multi-operator paymaster with Registry-based community management
- **AOA Mode** (PaymasterV4): Independent per-community paymasters deployed via EIP-1167 minimal proxy factory

### Key Technologies

- **Solidity 0.8.33** with Cancun EVM target
- **Foundry** for compilation, testing, and deployment
- **ERC-4337** Account Abstraction (EntryPoint v0.7)
- **UUPS Proxy Pattern** for upgradeable contracts
- **EIP-1167** Minimal Proxy for PaymasterV4 factory
- **Chainlink Price Feeds** for token pricing
- **Echidna** for fuzz testing
- **The Graph** for event indexing (subgraph)

---

## Project Structure

```
.
├── contracts/
│   ├── src/                    # Core Solidity contracts
│   │   ├── core/               # Registry, GTokenStaking
│   │   ├── tokens/             # GToken, MySBT, xPNTs
│   │   ├── paymasters/         # SuperPaymaster (v3), PaymasterV4
│   │   ├── modules/            # BLSValidator, DVTValidator, ReputationSystem
│   │   ├── interfaces/         # All interface definitions
│   │   └── accounts/           # SimpleAccount, SimpleAccountFactory
│   ├── test/                   # Foundry test files (*.t.sol)
│   │   ├── v3/                 # V3 contract tests
│   │   ├── v4/                 # V4 contract tests
│   │   ├── paymasters/         # Paymaster-specific tests
│   │   └── modules/            # DVT/BLS validator tests
│   ├── script/                 # Forge deployment scripts (*.s.sol)
│   │   ├── v3/                 # V3 deployment orchestration
│   │   ├── v4/                 # V4 deployment scripts
│   │   ├── checks/             # Post-deployment verification scripts
│   │   └── deployment/         # Step-by-step deployment scripts
│   └── lib/                    # Dependencies (foundry-installed)
│       ├── openzeppelin-contracts
│       ├── chainlink-brownie-contracts
│       ├── solady
│       └── forge-std
├── script/                     # Root-level tooling
│   └── gasless-tests/          # E2E gasless transaction tests (JS)
├── deployments/                # Deployment configs (config.<network>.json)
├── abis/                       # Extracted ABI JSONs
├── singleton-paymaster/        # Git submodule - Pimlico paymaster base
├── subgraph/                   # The Graph indexing configuration
├── foundry.toml                # Foundry configuration
├── deploy-core                 # Main deployment script
├── run_full_regression.sh      # Full regression test suite
└── echidna*.yaml               # Fuzz testing configurations
```

---

## Build, Test, and Development Commands

### Building

```bash
# Build all contracts
forge build

# Build only V3 contracts (faster, excludes legacy versions)
forge build --profile v3-only

# Format Solidity code
forge fmt
```

### Testing

```bash
# Run all tests
forge test

# Run a specific test file
forge test --match-path contracts/test/v3/Registry.t.sol

# Run a specific test function
forge test --match-test testRegisterCommunity

# Run with verbose output (levels: -v, -vv, -vvv, -vvvv)
forge test -vvvv

# Run with gas report
forge test --gas-report

# Run coverage
forge coverage
```

### Fuzz Testing (Echidna)

```bash
# Standard fuzz test
echidna . --config echidna.yaml

# Extended run (longer timeout)
echidna . --config echidna-long-run.yaml

# All contracts
echidna . --config echidna-all-contracts.yaml
```

### Deployment

```bash
# Deploy core contracts to a target environment
./deploy-core <env>          # env: anvil, sepolia, op-sepolia, optimism
./deploy-core anvil --force  # Force redeploy even if code unchanged

# Prepare test accounts after deployment
./prepare-test <env>

# Full regression (deploy + verification)
./run_full_regression.sh
./run_full_regression.sh --env sepolia --force

# Check deployed contract versions on-chain
./version-check-onchain.sh
```

### E2E Testing (Gasless Transactions)

```bash
# After deployment and prepare-test, run E2E tests
cd script/gasless-tests && ./run-all-tests.sh
```

### ABI Management

```bash
# Sync ABIs to SDK
./sync_to_sdk.sh
```

---

## Code Style Guidelines

### Solidity Conventions

- **Solidity Version**: 0.8.33 (strict pragma)
- **EVM Target**: Cancun
- **Style**: Follow `forge fmt` output
- **Comments**: All comments in English
- **License**: SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT

### Contract Versioning

All contracts MUST implement a `version()` function returning a semantic version string:

```solidity
function version() external pure virtual returns (string memory) {
    return "ContractName-X.Y.Z";
}
```

Examples:
- `Registry-4.1.0`
- `SuperPaymaster-3.2.2`
- `PaymasterV4-4.3.0`

### Naming Conventions

- **Contracts**: PascalCase (e.g., `SuperPaymaster`, `GTokenStaking`)
- **Functions**: camelCase (e.g., `validatePaymasterUserOp`)
- **Constants**: UPPER_SNAKE_CASE (e.g., `ROLE_COMMUNITY`, `ENTRY_POINT_V07`)
- **Storage Variables**: camelCase with meaningful prefixes
- **Events**: PascalCase with descriptive names (e.g., `CommunityRegistered`)
- **Errors**: PascalCase with descriptive names (e.g., `InvalidRoleConfiguration`)

### Import Order

```solidity
// 1. SPDX and pragma
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity 0.8.33;

// 2. External dependencies (OpenZeppelin, Chainlink)
import "@openzeppelin-v5.0.2/contracts/access/Ownable.sol";
import "@chainlink/contracts/src/v0.8/interfaces/AggregatorV3Interface.sol";

// 3. Internal interfaces
import "../interfaces/v3/IRegistry.sol";

// 4. Internal contracts/libraries
import "../core/GTokenStaking.sol";
```

### Remappings (from foundry.toml)

```
@openzeppelin/contracts/   → contracts/lib/openzeppelin-contracts/contracts/
@openzeppelin-v5.0.2/     → singleton-paymaster/lib/openzeppelin-contracts-v5.0.2/
@account-abstraction-v7/  → singleton-paymaster/lib/account-abstraction-v7/contracts/
account-abstraction/       → lib/account-abstraction/contracts/
@chainlink/contracts/      → contracts/lib/chainlink-brownie-contracts/contracts/
solady/                    → contracts/lib/solady/src/
src/                       → contracts/src/
```

---

## Testing Guidelines

### Test File Organization

- Test files end with `.t.sol`
- Test functions start with `test` (e.g., `testRegisterCommunity`)
- Fuzz tests use `invariant` or `fuzz` prefixes
- Place tests in appropriate subdirectories matching `contracts/src/` structure

### Test Structure

```solidity
contract MyContractTest is Test {
    MyContract contract;
    
    function setUp() public {
        // Deploy contracts
        contract = new MyContract();
    }
    
    function test_SpecificScenario() public {
        // Arrange
        vm.prank(user);
        
        // Act
        contract.doSomething();
        
        // Assert
        assertEq(result, expected);
    }
}
```

### Key Testing Patterns

- Use `vm.prank()` for single-call impersonation
- Use `vm.startPrank()` / `vm.stopPrank()` for multi-call impersonation
- Use `vm.expectRevert()` for testing revert conditions
- Use `vm.expectEmit()` for testing event emissions
- Use `vm.warp()` for timestamp manipulation
- Use `vm.roll()` for block number manipulation

### Test Categories

1. **Unit Tests**: Individual contract functionality
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     2	// AAStar.io contribution with love from 2023
     3	pragma solidity 0.8.33;
     4	import "@openzeppelin-v5.0.2/contracts/access/Ownable.sol";
     5	import "@openzeppelin-v5.0.2/contracts/utils/ReentrancyGuard.sol";
     6	import "src/interfaces/v3/IRegistry.sol";
     7	import "src/interfaces/v3/IGTokenStaking.sol";
     8	import "src/interfaces/IVersioned.sol";
     9	import {BLS} from "src/utils/BLS.sol";

    11	/// @notice Local sub-view of Registry used to fetch the staking pointer at
    12	///         verification time. We cast `REGISTRY` to this narrower interface
    13	///         rather than baking another constructor arg, so existing deploy
    14	///         scripts (4 in production + multiple archives) keep their 3-arg
    15	///         BLSAggregator construction unchanged. Mocks in the test suite
    16	///         already implement this view (set via `setStakingAddr`).
    17	interface IRegistryStakingAwareBLS {
    18	    function GTOKEN_STAKING() external view returns (IGTokenStaking);
    19	}

    23	        WARNING,
    24	        MINOR,
    25	        MAJOR
    26	    }
    27	    function queueSlash(address operator) external;
    28	    function executeSlashWithBLS(address operator, SlashLevel level, bytes calldata proof) external;
    29	}

    31	interface IDVTValidator {

    33	    function nextProposalId() external view returns (uint256);
    34	    function BLS_AGGREGATOR() external view returns (address);

    38	/// @notice Narrow sub-view of GTokenStaking exposing only the directed
    39	///         role-lock slash. `IGTokenStaking` (used elsewhere here for
    40	///         `roleLocks`) does not surface `slashByDVT`, so we cast the same
    41	///         staking pointer to this local interface rather than widening the
    42	///         shared interface (which every mock/implementer would then have to
    43	///         satisfy). The 5.5.0 deploy paths do NOT put the aggregator in
    44	///         `authorizedSlashers` (guardian slash is off while fraudProofVerifier == 0);
    45	///         enabling it grants the role in the same timelock batch as proposeFraudProofVerifier.
    46	interface IGTokenStakingSlash {
    47	    function slashByDVT(address operator, bytes32 roleId, uint256 penaltyAmount, string calldata reason) external;
    48	}
    49	
    50	/// @notice External, DVT-supplied fraud-proof verifier (Protocol B stage 2).
    51	///         Returns true iff `fraudProofId` proves the referenced proposal was
    52	///         fraudulent AND `guiltyGuardians` are exactly the co-signers to blame.
    53	///         Guardians are identified by ADDRESS, not slot: slots are reassignable
    54	///         (revokeBLSPublicKey frees a slot for reuse), so a slot captured at
    55	///         fraud time could later resolve to an innocent validator. Address
    56	///         binding is stable and is what the slash reads.
    57	///         BLSAggregator treats this as the sole authority on "who colluded";
    58	///         it does not itself judge fraud. Kept behind an interface so the
    59	///         detection layer can evolve without touching this contract.
    60	///
    61	///         CC-48 round-2: `domainDigest` is supplied by the aggregator and equals
    62	///         `BLSAggregator.fraudProofDigest(fraudProofId, guiltyGuardians)` =
    63	///         keccak256(abi.encode(domainSeparator(), TAG_FRAUD_PROOF, fraudProofId,
    64	///         guiltyGuardians)). It binds the proof to (versioned domain name, chainid,
    65	///         this aggregator, its Registry), so a fraud proof accepted on one
    66	///         aggregator/chain is not byte-valid on another. Verifiers MUST bind
    67	///         `domainDigest` into whatever they check; ignoring it re-opens
    68	///         cross-contract replay.
    69	///
    70	///         CC-48 round-5 (MEDIUM-1) — EXACT-SET BINDING IS PART OF THE CONTRACT.
    71	///         A verifier MUST return true only for the guardian set the evidence commits
    72	///         to, EXACTLY. A strict SUBSET, a SUPERSET, and any unrelated set must all be
    73	///         rejected for the same `(fraudProofId, fraudProof)`. "Every listed address is
    74	///         provably a co-signer" is NOT sufficient — that predicate is self-consistent
    75	///         on any subset, and evidence-checking verifiers (as opposed to the
    76	///         attester-commitment reference implementation) satisfy it by construction.
    77	///
    78	///         Why this is a security property and not a nicety: `fraudProofId` is
    79	///         SINGLE-USE FOR EVER. `guardianSlashCases[id].status != 0` blocks re-opening,
    80	///         and status 2 (executed) and 3 (expired) block it just as permanently as 1
    81	///         (pending). `queueGuardianSlash` is permissionless and the guardian set is
    82	///         chosen by the CALLER. So if a verifier accepts subsets, a colluder who sees
    83	///         an honest watcher's `queueGuardianSlash(id, {A,B,C}, proof)` in the mempool
    84	///         can front-run it with `queueGuardianSlash(id, {A}, proof)`: the case opens on
    85	///         {A} alone, executes, burns `id` permanently, and B and C become immune to
    86	///         that evidence while the chain records the matter as settled.
    87	///
    88	///         The gate for this property is
    89	///         `contracts/test/helpers/FraudProofVerifierConformance.sol` —
    90	///         `assertDomainBound` (domain) + `assertSetBound` (set completeness). Both must
    91	///         pass in the DVT repo's own CI before `fraudProofVerifier` leaves address(0).
    92	interface IFraudProofVerifier {
    93	    function verify(
    94	        bytes32 domainDigest,
    95	        uint256 fraudProofId,
    96	        address[] calldata guiltyGuardians,
    97	        bytes calldata fraudProof
    98	    ) external view returns (bool);
    99	}
   100	
   101	/**
   102	 * @title BLSAggregator
   103	 * @notice BLS signature aggregation and verification for DVT slash consensus (V3)
   104	 * @dev Aggregates signatures and updates global reputation in Registry V3.
   105	 *
   106	 *      P0-1 (B6-C1a): pkAgg is no longer accepted from the caller. Pre-fix
   107	 *      `verify(message, signerMask, pkAgg, sig)` accepted any caller-supplied
   108	 *      pkAgg, and the pairing equation `e(pk_agg, H(m)) == e(g1, sig)` is
   109	 *      mathematically satisfiable for any chosen pair (sig, pkAgg). This
   110	 *      allowed an anonymous attacker to forge BLS proofs against any operator
   111	 *      and trigger slash / blacklist / reputation actions. The fix:
   112	 *      1. Public keys are stored as typed `BLS.G1Point` (uncompressed, 128
   113	 *         bytes) along with a 1-indexed validator slot in `[1..MAX_VALIDATORS]`.
   114	 *      2. The aggregator reconstructs `pkAgg` itself from the on-chain
   115	 *         `blsPublicKeys` selected by `signerMask` using the EIP-2537
   116	 *         `BLS12_G1ADD` precompile.
   117	 *      3. The proof payload no longer contains a public key field; it is
   118	 *         strictly `abi.encode(signerMask, sigG2)` (msgG2 is also derived
   119	 *         on-chain via `BLS.hashToG2(expectedMessageHash)`).
   120	 *
   121	 *      Companion fix: `BLSValidator.sol` and `IBLSValidator.sol` are deleted
   122	 *      because the same forgery surface existed there. All callers (Registry,
   123	 *      ReputationSystem, DVTValidator) are routed through this aggregator.
   124	 */
   125	contract BLSAggregator is Ownable, ReentrancyGuard, IVersioned {
   126	    // ====================================
   127	    // Structs
   128	    // ====================================
   129	
   130	    /// @notice Stored BLS validator key. Format is uncompressed EIP-2537 G1
   131	    ///         (4 × 32 = 128 bytes) so the key can be fed directly to the
   132	    ///         G1ADD precompile during `_reconstructPkAgg` without a costly
   133	    ///         decompression step.
   134	    struct BLSValidatorKey {
   135	        BLS.G1Point publicKey;
   136	        uint8 index; // 1-indexed slot in [1..MAX_VALIDATORS]; 0 = unregistered
   137	        bool isActive;
   138	    }
   139	
   140	    struct AggregatedSignature {
   141	        bytes aggregatedSig; // 256 bytes G2
   142	        address[] signers;
   143	        bytes32 messageHash;
   144	        uint256 timestamp;
   145	        bool verified;
   146	    }
   147	
   148	    struct GuardianSlashCase {
   149	        bytes32 guardiansHash;
   150	        // CC-48 round-4 HIGH: the VERDICT is frozen, not the judge. `queueGuardianSlash`
   151	        // runs the verifier exactly once and stores keccak256(fraudProof) here; every
   152	        // later execute/retry re-presents the same proof bytes and is checked against
   153	        // this hash instead of re-asking the verifier.
   154	        //
   155	        // Round-3 froze `verifier` (the address) and re-verified against it. That is not
   156	        // enough: a UUPS/Transparent/Beacon verifier keeps its address AND its
   157	        // extcodehash across an implementation swap, so governance (or a compromised
   158	        // proxy admin) could still flip an already-queued case to `false` and run it out
   159	        // to expiry — the exact round-3 timing attack, one indirection deeper. No
   160	        // on-chain check on the *address* (code.length, extcodehash, EXTCODECOPY) can
   161	        // prove the semantics behind it are immutable, so the address stopped being the
   162	        // thing we rely on. Freezing the verdict also makes the case immune to the
   163	        // verifier selfdestructing, being upgraded to a reverting implementation, or
   164	        // simply going down between a partial execution and its retry.
   165	        bytes32 fraudProofHash;
   166	        uint64 deadline;
   167	        uint8 status; // 0=none, 1=pending, 2=executed, 3=expired
   168	        // CC-48 HIGH-2: a case is only "executed" once EVERY accused guardian has
   169	        // been individually resolved. Partial progress keeps status == 1 so the
   170	        // case stays retryable instead of collapsing into an all-or-nothing batch.
   171	        uint16 guardianCount;
   172	        uint16 resolvedCount;
   173	        /// @dev The slash fraction FROZEN AT QUEUE TIME, in basis points.
   174	        ///      The verdict was already frozen here (guardiansHash, fraudProofHash,
   175	        ///      deadline, verifier) and the price was not: `executeGuardianSlash` is
   176	        ///      permissionless and `setGuardianSlashBps` is immediate, so the caller's
   177	        ///      choice of moment picked which policy applied. Setting 10000 between
   178	        ///      queue and execute reinstated exactly the take-everything behaviour this
   179	        ///      cap exists to remove, on a case adjudicated under a 30% policy; setting
   180	        ///      100 softened a queued verdict 30x. A verdict's price is part of the
   181	        ///      verdict. Packs into the slot with deadline/status/counts at no cost.
   182	        ///      Found by pr-daemon and Codex.
   183	        uint16 slashBps;
   184	        // AUDIT ONLY as of round-4: the verifier that authorized this case, recorded so
   185	        // an observer can attribute the verdict. Execution no longer calls it, and no
   186	        // code path reads it for a decision — a rotation, upgrade, or selfdestruct after
   187	        // queueing changes nothing about the case's fate.
   188	        address verifier;
   189	    }
   190	
   191	    struct GuardianExitRequest {
   192	        uint64 readyAt;
   193	        uint64 expiresAt;
   194	    }
   195	
   196	    // ====================================
   197	    // Storage
   198	    // ====================================
   199	
   200	    IRegistry public immutable REGISTRY;
   201	    address public SUPERPAYMASTER;
   202	    address public DVT_VALIDATOR;
   203	
   204	    /// @notice Validator → registered key. `isActive` doubles as registration flag.
   205	    mapping(address => BLSValidatorKey) internal _blsKeys;
   206	
   207	    /// @notice 1-indexed slot → validator address. signerMask bit `i` (0-indexed)
   208	    ///         corresponds to validator at slot `i+1`.
   209	    mapping(uint8 => address) public validatorAtSlot;
   210	
   211	    mapping(uint256 => AggregatedSignature) public aggregatedSignatures;
   212	    mapping(uint256 => bool) public executedProposals;
   213	    /// @notice Replay guard for queueSlashWithConsensus. A queue proof commits to
   214	    ///         (operator, slashLevel, epoch, chainid); once consumed it cannot be
   215	    ///         replayed — otherwise the same signed proof could re-flag an operator
   216	    ///         after the owner cancelled the slash or after it already executed
   217	    ///         (a reusable withdraw-block DoS). A fresh, legitimate re-queue simply
   218	    ///         uses a new epoch (→ new hash).
   219	    mapping(bytes32 => bool) public usedSlashQueueHashes;
   220	    mapping(uint256 => uint256) public proposalNonces;
   221	
   222	    uint256 public minThreshold = 3; // Floor for the GENERIC executeProposal path only
   223	    uint256 public defaultThreshold = 7; // Default for the reputation consensus path
   224	    uint256 public constant MAX_VALIDATORS = 64;
   225	
   226	    /// @notice Absolute signature floor enforced inside `_checkSignatures` for
   227	    ///         EVERY verification path (a hard safety net; no path may verify
   228	    ///         below this). The generic executeProposal path layers the stricter
   229	    ///         `minThreshold` on top, so lowering the slash floor to 2 (for a
   230	    ///         2-of-3 WARNING) does NOT widen the generic path — that stays at
   231	    ///         minThreshold. Also the min a slash-table entry may be set to.
   232	    uint8 public constant SLASH_THRESHOLD_FLOOR = 2;
   233	
   234	    // ====================================
   235	    // CC-48 round-2: versioned domain separation
   236	    // ====================================
   237	    //
   238	    // Every signed pre-image on every path is
   239	    //     keccak256(abi.encode(domainSeparator(), <PATH_TAG>, ...fields))
   240	    // where
   241	    //     domainSeparator() = keccak256(abi.encode(DOMAIN_NAME, block.chainid,
   242	    //                                              address(this), address(REGISTRY)))
   243	    //
   244	    // Before this change a pre-image committed to chainid only. Two aggregators
   245	    // deployed on the SAME chain over the SAME validator keys/slots therefore
   246	    // accepted byte-identical proofs — an experiment-only deployment's signatures
   247	    // replayed verbatim against a production one. Refusing to *configure* the
   248	    // experimental contract is a policy control, not a cryptographic one; the
   249	    // domain now carries `address(this)` and the bound Registry, so a proof simply
   250	    // does not verify anywhere else. Registry re-derives the identical separator
   251	    // locally (see Registry.blsDomainSeparator) — it never asks the aggregator for
   252	    // it, keeping the second verification independent.
   253	    //
   254	    // Bump the *_v1 suffixes ONLY on a breaking encoding change; DVT/SDK pin them.
   255	
   256	    /// @notice Versioned domain name shared by every BLS pre-image in this system.
   257	    bytes32 public constant DOMAIN_NAME = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLSConsensus.v1");
   258	
   259	    /// @notice Path tags. Distinct per path so a proof for one path is never a
   260	    ///         valid proof for another, even with otherwise-identical fields.
   261	    bytes32 public constant TAG_QUEUE_SLASH = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLS.QueueSlash.v1");
   262	    bytes32 public constant TAG_EXECUTE_SLASH = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLS.ExecuteSlash.v1");
   263	    bytes32 public constant TAG_REPUTATION = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLS.Reputation.v1");
   264	    bytes32 public constant TAG_PROPOSAL = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLS.Proposal.v1");
   265	    bytes32 public constant TAG_POP = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLS.PoP.v1");
   266	    bytes32 public constant TAG_SIGNERS_COMMITMENT = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLS.SignersCommitment.v1");
   267	    bytes32 public constant TAG_FRAUD_PROOF = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLS.FraudProof.v1");
   268	
   269	    /// @notice The domain separator every pre-image on this contract commits to.
   270	    /// @dev    Not cached in storage: `block.chainid` must stay live so a chain
   271	    ///         fork cannot inherit the pre-fork domain, and both addresses are
   272	    ///         immutable anyway, so there is nothing to cache.
   273	    function domainSeparator() public view returns (bytes32) {
   274	        return keccak256(abi.encode(DOMAIN_NAME, block.chainid, address(this), address(REGISTRY)));
   275	    }
   276	
   277	    /// @notice Canonical digest handed to `IFraudProofVerifier.verify`.
   278	    /// @dev    Public so DVT can reproduce it byte-for-byte off-chain.
   279	    function fraudProofDigest(uint256 fraudProofId, address[] calldata guiltyGuardians) public view returns (bytes32) {
   280	        return keccak256(abi.encode(domainSeparator(), TAG_FRAUD_PROOF, fraudProofId, guiltyGuardians));
   281	    }
   282	
   283	    /// @notice Canonical proof-of-possession digest for (validator, publicKey).
   284	    /// @dev    CC-48 round-2 HIGH: the pre-image now binds the VALIDATOR ADDRESS
   285	    ///         (and the domain). Previously it covered only the public key, so the
   286	    ///         PoP sitting in one registrant's public calldata could be lifted by
   287	    ///         any other ROLE_DVT address to register the same key at a second slot.
   288	    ///         With the same key in N slots the reconstructed pkAgg is N*pk, and the
   289	    ///         single key holder can produce N*sk*H(m) — one signer masquerading as
   290	    ///         a quorum. Address binding kills the lift; `blsKeyOwner` (below) kills
   291	    ///         the duplicate registration even when the key holder tries it himself.
   292	    function popDigest(address validator, BLS.G1Point calldata publicKey) public view returns (bytes32) {
   293	        return keccak256(
   294	            abi.encode(
   295	                domainSeparator(), TAG_POP, validator, publicKey.x_a, publicKey.x_b, publicKey.y_a, publicKey.y_b
   296	            )
   297	        );
   298	    }
   299	
   300	    /// @notice H-1: privileged selectors the GENERIC executeProposal path may never invoke
   301	    ///         on any target (caller-chosen threshold >= minThreshold, no proposalFloor):
   302	    ///         - SP queueSlash / executeSlashWithBLS and Registry/DVT markProposalExecuted:
   303	    ///           slash and id-marking go through verifyAndExecute / queueSlashWithConsensus.
   304	    ///         - Registry.batchUpdateGlobalReputation: Registry re-verifies the proof but its
   305	    ///           domain does not change on DVT rotation and it has no floor, so a generic call
   306	    ///           could execute a pre-rotation reputation proof. Reputation goes through the
   307	    ///           verifyAndExecute reputation branch, which enforces proposalFloor.
   308	    ///         - GTokenStaking slash / slashByDVT: an authorized slasher could otherwise burn
   309	    ///           any stake by any amount, bypassing severity bps, cooldown and floor.
   310	    ///         Whole targets are NOT blocked: Registry.updateOperatorBlacklist (own proof,
   311	    ///         defaultThreshold, blacklistNonce) and SP.updatePriceDVT (freshness, ±20% bands)
   312	    ///         are legitimate generic actions. scripts/check_generic_selector_coverage.py (CI)
   313	    ///         classifies every privileged selector reachable from this path.
   314	    bytes4 private constant SEL_QUEUE_SLASH = bytes4(keccak256("queueSlash(address)"));
   315	    bytes4 private constant SEL_EXECUTE_SLASH_BLS = bytes4(keccak256("executeSlashWithBLS(address,uint8,bytes)"));
   316	    bytes4 private constant SEL_MARK_PROPOSAL_EXECUTED = bytes4(keccak256("markProposalExecuted(uint256)"));
   317	    bytes4 private constant SEL_BATCH_REPUTATION =
   318	        bytes4(keccak256("batchUpdateGlobalReputation(uint256,address[],uint256[],uint256,bytes)"));
   319	    bytes4 private constant SEL_STAKING_SLASH = bytes4(keccak256("slash(address,uint256,string)"));
   320	    bytes4 private constant SEL_STAKING_SLASH_BY_DVT = bytes4(keccak256("slashByDVT(address,bytes32,uint256,string)"));
   321	
   322	    /// @notice Per-severity slash consensus threshold, keyed by SlashLevel
   323	    ///         (0=WARNING, 1=MINOR, 2=MAJOR). Bootstrap (N=3): 2/3/3. This
   324	    ///         replaces the flat defaultThreshold for the slash-only path so the
   325	    ///         bar scales with severity and with the validator set over time,
   326	    ///         by governance table update rather than a code change.
   327	    mapping(uint8 => uint8) public slashThresholds;
   328	
   329	    /// @notice Address permitted to update `slashThresholds`. Set this to a
   330	    ///         multisig for plain governance, or to a TimelockController(multisig)
   331	    ///         to get timelocked policy changes WITHOUT building timelock logic
   332	    ///         (and its bytecode) into this contract. Owner rotates it.
   333	    address public slashPolicyAdmin;
   334	
   335	    /// @notice Fraction of a colluding guardian's remaining ROLE_DVT lock burned per
   336	    ///         `executeGuardianSlash`, in basis points.
   337	    /// @dev    WHY THIS EXISTS. Before it, this path passed the guardian's ENTIRE remaining
   338	    ///         lock to `slashByDVT`, so `lock.amount == 0` was hit on every successful
   339	    ///         guardian slash and `_removeUserRole` fired every time: the contract could not
   340	    ///         express a partial slash at all. With registrations sitting exactly at
   341	    ///         `minStake` and the MINOR threshold equal to the registered count, one slash
   342	    ///         therefore dropped the slash quorum below its own threshold — the punishment
   343	    ///         mechanism disabling the mechanism. Over-staking could not fix that while the
   344	    ///         penalty was defined as "everything left".
   345	    ///
   346	    ///         WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT FIX. A fraction makes over-staking meaningful,
   347	    ///         and that is all it does. It does NOT on its own stop the quorum from
   348	    ///         disabling itself, because the eligibility gate is an absolute comparison
   349	    ///         against `minStake`, not a relative one:
   350	    ///
   351	    ///           stake == minStake  (the CURRENT deployment: every ROLE_DVT lock is
   352	    ///                               exactly 30e18)      -> 30% slash leaves 0.7x minStake
   353	    ///                                                      -> still below the gate
   354	    ///                                                      -> still ejected
   355	    ///                                                      -> quorum still collapses
   356	    ///           stake >= minStake / (1 - bps)  (>= ~1.43x, i.e. 43e18 for a 30e18 gate)
   357	    ///                                              -> survives one slash, stays in quorum
   358	    ///
   359	    ///         So the cap is NECESSARY and NOT SUFFICIENT. The other half is an operational
   360	    ///         rule — stake above `minStake` — which nothing in this contract enforces.
   361	    ///         `GuardianSlashE2E` pins both halves: the margin-0 case still ejects, and the
   362	    ///         over-staked case does not. Do not read this parameter as having solved it.
   363	    ///
   364	    ///         It also does NOT make a guardian un-slashable: repeated collusion walks the
   365	    ///         balance down, and each case is a separate `fraudProofId`.
   366	    uint16 public guardianSlashBps;
   367	
   368	    /// @dev Fat-finger bounds, not policy. 0 would make the path a no-op that still marks
   369	    ///      the case resolved; above 100% is meaningless. Ops pick the real value.
   370	    uint16 public constant GUARDIAN_SLASH_BPS_MIN = 100; // 1%
   371	    uint16 public constant GUARDIAN_SLASH_BPS_MAX = 10000; // 100%
   372	    uint16 internal constant _BPS_DENOMINATOR = 10000;
   373	
   374	    /// @notice H-02: when true, a staked ROLE_DVT validator may self-register their OWN
   375	    ///         BLS key (with proof-of-possession) instead of requiring an owner call.
   376	    ///         Default false — onboarding stays owner-gated (off-chain trust established
   377	    ///         first) until governance flips it on. Closes the otherwise-inconsistent
   378	    ///         path where Registry ROLE_DVT is permissionless (self-service stake) but
   379	    ///         BLS-key registration here was owner-only.
   380	    bool public permissionlessBLSRegistration;
   381	
   382	    /// @notice DVT-supplied fraud-proof verifier for guardian-collusion slashing
   383	    ///         (Protocol B stage 2). address(0) = feature dormant: NO NEW CASE can be
   384	    ///         opened, because `queueGuardianSlash` — the only way to open one — reverts
   385	    ///         with `FraudProofVerifierNotSet`. It does NOT stop a case that is already
   386	    ///         queued: since round-4 the verdict is frozen at queue time, so
   387	    ///         `executeGuardianSlash` never reads this slot at all (CC-48 round-5
   388	    ///         MEDIUM-2: the pre-round-5 NatSpec claimed the opposite and would have led
   389	    ///         an operator to believe zeroing this halts an in-flight slash).
   390	    ///         This is the ONE seam the detection layer plugs into; the aggregator never
   391	    ///         judges fraud itself.
   392	    address public fraudProofVerifier;
   393	
   394	    /// @notice Per-(fraudProofId, guardian) slash record for executeGuardianSlash.
   395	    ///         Consumption is tracked PER GUARDIAN — and only for guardians actually
   396	    ///         slashed — so submitting an already-exited co-signer can never burn the
   397	    ///         proof for the still-staked colluders (the global-id-consumption flaw this
   398	    ///         replaces). Own id-space; never collides with slash/reputation proposalIds.
   399	    mapping(uint256 => mapping(address => bool)) public guardianSlashed;
   400	
   401	    /// @notice A' attribution (CC-89 stage-2): commitment to the exact signer
   402	    ///         ADDRESS set of each executed proposal, snapshotted at execution time
   403	    ///         before any revokeBLSPublicKey can reassign a slot. A fraud-proof
   404	    ///         verifier matches proof-supplied claimedSigners against this to
   405	    ///         attribute a fraudulent slash to real addresses. It is a 1-slot
   406	    ///         fingerprint and is NOT reversible — the address list's data
   407	    ///         availability is the DVT detection layer's (redundant watchers) job.
   408	    mapping(uint256 => bytes32) public proposalSignersCommitment;
   409	
   410	    /// @notice Two-step guardian-slash lifecycle. Queueing a verifier-approved
   411	    ///         case freezes every accused guardian's ROLE_DVT exit in Registry;
   412	    ///         execution or permissionless expiry releases exactly one count.
   413	    mapping(uint256 => GuardianSlashCase) public guardianSlashCases;
   414	    mapping(address => GuardianExitRequest) public guardianExitRequests;
   415	    mapping(address => uint256) public pendingGuardianSlashCount;
   416	
   417	    /// @notice CC-48 HIGH-2: per-(case, guardian) release marker. Set exactly once,
   418	    ///         when that guardian's `pendingGuardianSlashCount` contribution for the
   419	    ///         case is given back — either because the slash succeeded, because the
   420	    ///         guardian had nothing left to slash, or because the case expired. A
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     2	// AAStar.io contribution with love from 2023
     3	pragma solidity 0.8.33;
     4	import "@openzeppelin-v5.0.2/contracts/access/Ownable.sol";
     5	import "src/interfaces/v3/IRegistry.sol";
     6	import "src/interfaces/v3/IBLSAggregator.sol";
     7	import "src/interfaces/v3/IGTokenStaking.sol";
     8	import "src/interfaces/IVersioned.sol";
     9	import {BLS} from "src/utils/BLS.sol";
    10	import "src/interfaces/IEvidenceCategory.sol";
    11	
    12	/// @notice Local sub-view of Registry exposing the staking pointer. Kept here
    13	///         (rather than on IRegistry) so existing mocks across the test suite
    14	///         do not need to implement the GTOKEN_STAKING getter — DVTValidator
    15	///         simply casts its registry reference to this narrower interface.
    16	interface IRegistryStakingAware {
    17	    function GTOKEN_STAKING() external view returns (IGTokenStaking);
    18	}
    19	
    20	interface IBLSProposalFloor {
    21	    function proposalFloor() external view returns (uint256);
    22	}
    23	
    24	interface IBLSKeyRevoker {
    25	    function revokeBLSPublicKey(address validator) external;
    26	}

    28	interface IBLSKeyRegistration {
    29	    function owner() external view returns (address);
    30	    function suspended(address validator) external view returns (bool);
    31	    function permissionlessBLSRegistration() external view returns (bool);
    32	    function MAX_VALIDATORS() external view returns (uint256);
    33	    function validatorAtSlot(uint8 slot) external view returns (address);
    34	    function getBLSPublicKey(address validator) external view returns (BLS.G1Point memory, uint8, bool);
    35	    function registerBLSPublicKey(address validator, BLS.G1Point calldata key, uint8 slot, BLS.G2Point calldata pop) external;
    36	}
    37	
    38	/**
    39	 * @title DVTValidator
    40	 * @notice Distributed Validator Technology for operator monitoring (V3)
    41	 * @dev Manages slash proposals and coordinates with BLSAggregator.
    42	 *      Its owner must be the same owner as BLSAggregator: checked by DeployLive, by
    43	 *      owner-forwarded registerBLSPublicKey and by removeValidator; a later divergence
    44	 *      (transferOwnership on one side) disables both owner paths, fail-closed.
    45	 *      DVT rotation is an immediate owner operation; verifier rotation retains
    46	 *      BLSAggregator's contract-enforced delay.
    47	 *      Only the aggregator's current DVT can allocate proposal IDs. Each allocation
    48	 *      reads the aggregator's monotonic proposalFloor, captured at activation.
    49	 *      The aggregator enforces that floor on slash and aggregator-routed reputation
    50	 *      executed through the current DVT, including after reactivation. Stale proposals
    51	 *      need fresh IDs and proofs on this path. Owner-direct verifyAndExecute with
    52	 *      caller-chosen IDs, generic proposals, authorized direct Registry reputation
    53	 *      proofs and unused consensus queue pre-flags remain outside this execution floor.
    54	 *      Pre-flags have no automatic expiry, are owner-cancellable via
    55	 *      SuperPaymasterAdmin.cancelSlash and are one-shot per (operator, level, epoch).
    56	 */
    57	contract DVTValidator is Ownable, IVersioned {
    58	    bytes32 public constant TAG_EVIDENCE = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.DVT.Evidence.v2");
    59	    uint256 private constant EVIDENCE_CHECK_GAS = 100_000;
    60	
    61	    struct ValidatorInfo {
    62	        address validatorAddress;
    63	        bool isActive;
    64	    }
    65	
    66	    struct SlashProposal {
    67	        address operator;
    68	        uint8 slashLevel;
    69	        string reason;
    70	        bool executed;
    71	        // P0-4 fix: explicit existence flag so that operator==address(0) remains
    72	        // a valid proposal target. BLSAggregator intentionally supports zero-
    73	        // operator proposals (it skips slash when operator is zero), so using
    74	        // operator==address(0) as a non-existence sentinel breaks that path.
    75	        bool exists;
    76	        // Content-addressed hash of the off-chain evidence (audit record) that
    77	        // justified this proposal. Appended last to keep the pre-existing field
    78	        // indices stable. Bound into the BLS-signed message on the slash-only
    79	        // path so the on-chain slash is cryptographically tied to a specific
    80	        // evidence artifact. bytes32(0) = no evidence committed.
    81	        bytes32 evidenceHash;
    82	        // ✅ Removed validators[] and signatures[]
    83	        // Individual signatures collected off-chain via DVT P2P protocol
    84	        // Only aggregated BLS proof submitted on-chain
    85	    }
    86	
    87	    IRegistry public immutable REGISTRY;
    88	    address public BLS_AGGREGATOR;
    89	
    90	    mapping(address => bool) public isValidator;
    91	    mapping(uint256 => SlashProposal) public proposals;
    92	    uint256 private _nextProposalId;
    93	    mapping(bytes32 => address) public checkerOf;
    94	    bytes32[] private _categories;
    95	
    96	    event ProposalCreated(uint256 indexed id, address indexed operator, uint8 level);
    97	    event ProposalSigned(uint256 indexed id, address indexed validator);
    98	    event ProposalExecuted(uint256 indexed id);
    99	    /// @notice Emitted when local proposer admission is permissionlessly pruned because they
   100	    ///         no longer satisfy the role+stake requirements. `hadRole` and
   101	    ///         `stakeAmount` capture the on-chain reason at prune time so off-
   102	    ///         chain monitors can attribute the eviction.
   103	    event ValidatorPruned(address indexed v, bool hadRole, uint256 stakeAmount);
   104	    /// @notice Emitted when the owner force-removes a validator regardless of
   105	    ///         their current role/stake state.
   106	    event ValidatorRemoved(address indexed v);
   107	    /// @notice Emitted when the owner admits a proposer that was not admitted before.
   108	    event ValidatorAdded(address indexed v);
   109	    /// @notice Emitted when the BLS aggregator address is updated by the owner.
   110	    event BLSAggregatorUpdated(address indexed oldAddr, address indexed newAddr);
   111	
   112	    error NotValidator();
   113	    error AlreadySigned();
   114	    error ProposalExecutedAlready();
   115	    error OnlyBLSAggregator();
   116	    /// @notice Reverted when an action references a proposalId that has not been
   117	    ///         created via createProposal. Defense against pre-poison attacks
   118	    ///         where an adversary tricks the BLS aggregator path into marking
   119	    ///         an unborn proposalId as executed.
   120	    error ProposalDoesNotExist();
   121	    error NotAuthorizedExecutor();
   122	    /// @notice Reverted when addValidator is called for an address that does
   123	    ///         not hold ROLE_DVT in the Registry.
   124	    error ValidatorMissingRole();
   125	    /// @notice Reverted when addValidator is called for an address whose
   126	    ///         locked stake under ROLE_DVT is below the role's minStake.
   127	    error ValidatorStakeBelowMinimum(uint256 actual, uint256 required);
   128	    /// @notice Reverted when the Registry has no staking pointer wired up yet.
   129	    error StakingNotConfigured();
   130	    /// @notice Reverted when an operation references an address that is not
   131	    ///         locally admitted proposer (`isValidator[v] == false`); this flag
   132	    ///         is not required for permissionless BLS voting.
   133	    error NotActiveValidator(address v);
   134	    /// @notice Reverted when an active validator's ROLE_DVT has been revoked
   135	    ///         in Registry but the local `isValidator` flag is still set —
   136	    ///         the live-liveness check (P0 follow-up) catches this drift.
   137	    error ValidatorRoleRevoked(address v);
   138	    /// @notice Reverted when `pruneValidator` is called for a validator that
   139	    ///         still satisfies the role + stake requirements; only ineligible
   140	    ///         validators may be permissionlessly pruned.
   141	    error ValidatorStillEligible(address v);
   142	    error InvalidFirstProposalId(uint256 firstProposalId);
   143	    error ProposalIdSpaceExhausted();
   144	    error NotCurrentDVT();
   145	    error ValidatorSuspended(address validator);
   146	    error PermissionlessRegistrationDisabled();
   147	    error UnauthorizedCaller(address caller);
   148	    error SlotOutOfRange(uint8 slot);
   149	
   150	    error EvidenceConfigLengthMismatch();
   151	    error ZeroEvidenceCategory();
   152	    error ZeroEvidenceChecker();
   153	    error DuplicateEvidenceCategory(bytes32 category);
   154	    error UnknownEvidenceCategory(bytes32 category);
   155	    error EvidenceRejected(bytes32 category);
   156	    error EvidenceHashMismatch();
   157	    error ReputationEvidenceHashNotZero();
   158	    error ReputationEvidenceNotEmpty();
   159	    error MissingEvidenceHash();
   160	    error ProposalShapeMismatch();
   161	
   162	    /// @param firstProposalId Non-zero lower bound for this deployment's IDs, below 2^128.
   163	    ///        Allocation uses max(this, aggregator.proposalFloor()) (nextProposalId), so a
   164	    ///        value below the floor is lifted, not rejected.
   165	    constructor(address _registry, uint256 firstProposalId, bytes32[] memory categories, address[] memory checkers)
   166	        Ownable(msg.sender)
   167	    {
   168	        if (firstProposalId == 0 || firstProposalId >> 128 != 0) {
   169	            revert InvalidFirstProposalId(firstProposalId);
   170	        }
   171	        if (categories.length != checkers.length) revert EvidenceConfigLengthMismatch();
   172	        for (uint256 i; i < categories.length; ++i) {
   173	            bytes32 category = categories[i];
   174	            address checker = checkers[i];
   175	            if (category == bytes32(0)) revert ZeroEvidenceCategory();
   176	            if (checker == address(0)) revert ZeroEvidenceChecker();
   177	            if (checkerOf[category] != address(0)) revert DuplicateEvidenceCategory(category);
   178	            checkerOf[category] = checker;
   179	            _categories.push(category);
   180	        }
   181	        REGISTRY = IRegistry(_registry);
   182	        _nextProposalId = firstProposalId;
   183	    }
   184	
   185	    function version() external pure override returns (string memory) {
   186	        return "DVTValidator-0.9.0";
   187	    }
   188	
   189	    /// @notice Next allocatable ID, including one read of the retained aggregator's floor.
   190	    /// @dev Carry the floor through rotations even when this DVT never creates a proposal.
   191	    function nextProposalId() public view returns (uint256 id) {
   192	        id = _nextProposalId;
   193	        if (BLS_AGGREGATOR != address(0)) {
   194	            uint256 floor = IBLSProposalFloor(BLS_AGGREGATOR).proposalFloor();
   195	            if (id < floor) id = floor;
   196	        }
   197	    }
   198	
   199	    function categoryCount() external view returns (uint256) {
   200	        return _categories.length;
   201	    }
   202	
   203	    function categoryAt(uint256 index) external view returns (bytes32) {
   204	        return _categories[index];
   205	    }
   206	
   207	    /// @notice Restrict to BLS aggregator or registered DVT validators (P0-4)
   208	    modifier onlyAuthorizedExecutor() {
   209	        if (msg.sender != BLS_AGGREGATOR && !isValidator[msg.sender]) {
   210	            revert NotAuthorizedExecutor();
   211	        }
   212	        _;
   213	    }
   214	
   215	    /// @notice Register a new DVT validator after verifying both role and stake.
   216	    /// @dev    P0-2: Pre-V5.4 this was an unconditional `onlyOwner` write,
   217	    ///         meaning the owner could grow the quorum with stake-less keys
   218	    ///         and the entire DVT economic guarantee evaporated. Combined with
   219	    ///         the BLS forgery (P0-1) it left the consensus layer with no real
   220	    ///         skin-in-the-game backing.
   221	    ///
   222	    ///         The check reads minStake dynamically from
   223	    ///         `Registry.getRoleConfig(ROLE_DVT)` so governance can tune the
   224	    ///         floor via `Registry.configureRole` without redeploying. Initial
   225	    ///         deploy-time floor is 200 ether GToken (10x previous default).
   226	    /// @dev Local admission authorizes proposers; permissionless BLS voters need
   227	    ///      no local admission. Consensus calls re-check role, stake and suspension
   228	    ///      live. Pruning clears only this local flag. Admission never clears the
   229	    ///      aggregator's suspension; reinstatement is a separate owner operation.
   230	    function addValidator(address _v) external onlyOwner {
   231	        bytes32 roleDvt = keccak256("DVT");
   232	
   233	        if (!REGISTRY.hasRole(roleDvt, _v)) revert ValidatorMissingRole();
   234	
   235	        IGTokenStaking staking = IRegistryStakingAware(address(REGISTRY)).GTOKEN_STAKING();
   236	        if (address(staking) == address(0)) revert StakingNotConfigured();
   237	
   238	        IRegistry.RoleConfig memory cfg = REGISTRY.getRoleConfig(roleDvt);
   239	        (uint128 amount,,,,) = staking.roleLocks(_v, roleDvt);
   240	        if (uint256(amount) < cfg.minStake) {
   241	            revert ValidatorStakeBelowMinimum(uint256(amount), cfg.minStake);
   242	        }
   243	
   244	        if (!isValidator[_v]) {
   245	            isValidator[_v] = true;
   246	            emit ValidatorAdded(_v);
   247	        }
   248	    }
   249	
   250	    /// @notice Register a BLS key through the owner-curated or permissionless path.
   251	    /// @dev Only the aggregator owner can clear its authoritative suspension.
   252	    ///      Every registration, including owner forwarding, requires an address-bound PoP.
   253	    ///      Permissionless registration needs live role/stake, but no owner admission.
   254	    function registerBLSPublicKey(
   255	        address validator, BLS.G1Point calldata key, uint8 slot, BLS.G2Point calldata pop
   256	    ) external {
   257	        IBLSKeyRegistration aggregator = IBLSKeyRegistration(BLS_AGGREGATOR);
   258	        if (msg.sender != owner()) {
   259	            if (!aggregator.permissionlessBLSRegistration()) revert PermissionlessRegistrationDisabled();
   260	            if (msg.sender != validator) revert UnauthorizedCaller(msg.sender);
   261	            _requireEligibleValidator(validator);
   262	            uint8 maxValidators = uint8(aggregator.MAX_VALIDATORS());
   263	            if (slot == 0 || slot > maxValidators) revert SlotOutOfRange(slot);
   264	            (, uint8 existingSlot, bool active) = aggregator.getBLSPublicKey(validator);
   265	            if (active) {
   266	                slot = existingSlot;
   267	            } else {
   268	                slot = 1;
   269	                while (slot <= maxValidators && aggregator.validatorAtSlot(slot) != address(0)) ++slot;
   270	                if (slot > maxValidators) revert SlotOutOfRange(slot);
   271	            }
   272	        } else if (aggregator.owner() != msg.sender) {
   273	            // Owner forwarding needs the SHARED owner, as removeValidator does: if the DVT and
   274	            // aggregator owners diverge, the DVT owner must not keep curating the key set.
   275	            revert UnauthorizedCaller(msg.sender);
   276	        }
   277	        aggregator.registerBLSPublicKey(validator, key, slot, pop);
   278	    }
   279	
   280	    /// @notice Real-time validator liveness gate (P0 follow-up).
   281	    /// @dev    The legacy `addValidator` only checked role+stake at registration
   282	    ///         time. After that, exitRole / unlockAndTransfer / slash could leave
   283	    ///         a validator with zero stake or no ROLE_DVT, but `isValidator[v]`
   284	    ///         and the BLS slot stayed live — the operator could keep voting in
   285	    ///         quorums even though their economic backing was gone.
   286	    ///
   287	    ///         This helper re-validates the CALLER against Registry + Staking on proposal
   288	    ///         creation, queue forwarding and non-aggregator execution (signer eligibility is
   289	    ///         re-checked separately in BLSAggregator._reconstructPkAgg). It requires the local
   290	    ///         admission gate plus live suspension, role and stake checks:
   291	    ///           1. Local `isValidator[v]` flag still set.
   292	    ///           2. Registry still grants ROLE_DVT to `v` (covers explicit
   293	    ///              role revocation outside an exitRole flow).
   294	    ///           3. Locked GToken stake under ROLE_DVT >= role's `minStake`
   295	    ///              (covers exitRole, partial unlock, and slash drawdowns).
   296	    function _requireActiveValidator(address v) internal view {
   297	        if (!isValidator[v]) revert NotActiveValidator(v);
   298	        _requireEligibleValidator(v);
   299	    }
   300	
   301	    function _requireEligibleValidator(address v) internal view {
   302	        if (IBLSKeyRegistration(BLS_AGGREGATOR).suspended(v)) revert ValidatorSuspended(v);
   303	        bytes32 roleDvt = keccak256("DVT");
   304	        if (!REGISTRY.hasRole(roleDvt, v)) revert ValidatorRoleRevoked(v);
   305	        IGTokenStaking staking = IRegistryStakingAware(address(REGISTRY)).GTOKEN_STAKING();
   306	        if (address(staking) == address(0)) revert StakingNotConfigured();
   307	        IRegistry.RoleConfig memory cfg = REGISTRY.getRoleConfig(roleDvt);
   308	        (uint128 amount,,,,) = staking.roleLocks(v, roleDvt);
   309	        if (uint256(amount) < cfg.minStake) {
   310	            revert ValidatorStakeBelowMinimum(uint256(amount), cfg.minStake);
   311	        }
   312	    }
   313	
   314	    /// @notice Create a pure-reputation proposal without an evidence commitment.
   315	    function createProposal(address operator, uint8 level, string calldata reason) external returns (uint256 id) {
   316	        return _createProposal(operator, level, reason, bytes32(0));
   317	    }
   318	
   319	    /// @notice Create a proposal whose shape is fixed by operator and level.
   320	    /// @dev Only (operator == 0, level == 0) is reputation. Slash shapes require
   321	    ///      a configured category and nonzero commitment; the hash-only API cannot
   322	    ///      identify a category or run its checker until raw evidence is supplied.
   323	    ///      An empty allowlist reports UnknownEvidenceCategory(0) at creation.
   324	    /// @param  evidenceHash content-addressed hash of the audit record justifying
   325	    ///         the slash; bound into the BLS-signed message at execution time.
   326	    function createProposal(address operator, uint8 level, string calldata reason, bytes32 evidenceHash)
   327	        external
   328	        returns (uint256 id)
   329	    {
   330	        return _createProposal(operator, level, reason, evidenceHash);
   331	    }
   332	
   333	    function _createProposal(address operator, uint8 level, string calldata reason, bytes32 evidenceHash)
   334	        internal
   335	        returns (uint256 id)
   336	    {
   337	        // P0 follow-up: enforce live liveness before allowing a stake-less
   338	        // ex-validator to mint new proposals.
   339	        _requireActiveValidator(msg.sender);
   340	        if (IBLSAggregator(BLS_AGGREGATOR).DVT_VALIDATOR() != address(this)) revert NotCurrentDVT();
   341	        if (operator == address(0) && level == 0) {
   342	            if (evidenceHash != bytes32(0)) revert ReputationEvidenceHashNotZero();
   343	        } else {
   344	            if (_categories.length == 0) revert UnknownEvidenceCategory(bytes32(0));
   345	            if (evidenceHash == bytes32(0)) revert MissingEvidenceHash();
   346	        }
   347	        id = nextProposalId();
   348	        if (id >> 128 != 0) revert ProposalIdSpaceExhausted();
   349	        _nextProposalId = id + 1;
   350	        SlashProposal storage p = proposals[id];
   351	        p.operator = operator;
   352	        p.slashLevel = level;
   353	        p.reason = reason;
   354	        p.evidenceHash = evidenceHash;
   355	        // P0-4 fix: mark the proposal as created so existence checks use p.exists
   356	        // instead of operator==address(0), allowing zero-operator proposals.
   357	        p.exists = true;
   358	        // P0-17: explicit reset — auto-increment id implies executed defaults to
   359	        // false, but a hostile BLS aggregator path could have pre-set executed
   360	        // for an unborn id. Resetting here guarantees a freshly created proposal
   361	        // starts in an executable state.
   362	        p.executed = false;
   363	        emit ProposalCreated(id, operator, level);
   364	    }
   365	
   366	    /**
   367	     * @notice Mark proposal as executed (called by BLSAggregator after successful execution)
   368	     * @dev This is called after BLS proof verification succeeds
   369	     */
   370	    function markProposalExecuted(uint256 id) external {
   371	        if (msg.sender != BLS_AGGREGATOR) revert OnlyBLSAggregator();
   372	        SlashProposal storage p = proposals[id];
   373	        // P0-17: even though caller is gated to the BLS aggregator, defense-in-
   374	        // depth requires the targeted proposal to actually exist. Without this
   375	        // guard, a forged BLS proof (P0-1) would let the aggregator mark any
   376	        // future proposalId as executed, permanently DoS'ing it once createProposal
   377	        // assigns that id (the legitimate executeWithProof would revert on
   378	        // ProposalExecutedAlready).
   379	        // P0-4 fix: use p.exists instead of operator==address(0) — zero-operator
   380	        // proposals are valid in BLSAggregator (it skips slash for address(0)).
   381	        if (!p.exists) revert ProposalDoesNotExist();
   382	        p.executed = true;
   383	        emit ProposalExecuted(id);
   384	    }
   385	
   386	    // ✅ Removed signProposal and _forward functions
   387	    // DVT validators collect signatures off-chain via P2P protocol
   388	    // Last validator aggregates BLS signatures and calls executeWithProof
   389	
   390	    /**
   391	     * @notice Step 1 of the two-step slash: forward an aggregated queue proof to the
   392	     *         aggregator, which pre-flags the operator (SP.queueSlash) at the
   393	     *         per-severity threshold. Kept separate from execution so the flag lands
   394	     *         in an earlier tx (front-run protection). Not tied to a proposalId —
   395	     *         queue by (operator, level) so it can precede createProposal.
   396	     */
   397	    function queueSlashWithProof(
   398	        address operator,
   399	        uint8 slashLevel,
   400	        uint256 epoch,
   401	        bytes calldata evidence,
   402	        bytes calldata proof
   403	    ) external {

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Every privileged selector reachable from BLSAggregator.executeProposal is classified.
     3	
     4	The GENERIC executeProposal path calls any target with caller-chosen calldata at a
     5	caller-chosen threshold (>= minThreshold), with no proposalFloor. Whatever the
     6	aggregator is privileged to do somewhere, a generic proposal can do too, unless the
     7	selector is on the H-1 denylist (BLSAggregator.sol, SEL_* constants). The denylist
     8	missed entries three times (markProposalExecuted, then batchUpdateGlobalReputation,
     9	then GTokenStaking.slash / slashByDVT), so this check enumerates instead of trusting.
    10	
    11	Contracts enumerated: those where the aggregator holds a privileged identity.
    12	  - SuperPaymaster (+Admin): BLS_AGGREGATOR (initBLSAggregator / queue+applyBLSAggregator)
    13	  - Registry: blsAggregator (setBLSAggregator), and a reputation source
    14	    (DeployLive.s.sol: registry.setReputationSource(aggregator))
    15	  - GTokenStaking: authorizedSlashers. The 5.5.0 deploy paths (DeployLive, DeployAnvil, the Sepolia
    16	    manifest) grant NONE to the aggregator; older/E2E scripts still do (RegistryUpgradeBatchLib,
    17	    DeployBLSAggregatorSepolia, DeployRepCreditSepolia); kept here so any grant is covered.
    18	  - DVTValidator: BLS_AGGREGATOR (setBLSAggregator)
    19	  - ReputationSystem: trusts Registry.isReputationSource callers (setCommunityReputation)
    20	
    21	For every external/public non-view function in those ABIs (abis/*.json):
    22	  - if its body, a modifier it uses, or an internal function it calls (transitively, same
    23	    sources) references an aggregator identity, it MUST appear in CLASSIFIED below, as
    24	    "blocked" (and then in the denylist) or "allowed: <reason>";
    25	  - otherwise it is classified automatically as "no aggregator privilege": the aggregator
    26	    calling it is no different from any other caller.
    27	Limits (stated, #633 review): this is a source-text scan. It recognizes the spellings used today
    28	(the aggregator variable and local aliases of it, encodeCall payloads, multi-line calls, internal
    29	helper parameters, receivers mapped in RECEIVERS) - it is NOT a general guarantee. A grant written
    30	some other way, gates in base classes or external callbacks, and contracts outside TARGETS can
    31	escape it. The general guarantee is the state-based step-13 readback (OWNER-NOT-AGGREGATOR,
    32	authorizedSlashers(aggregator) == false, the reputation-source set, AGG-ZERO-BALANCES), which does
    33	not depend on how a deploy script is written. The ABIs come from abis/, which PR-B's ABI gate keeps
    34	equal to a fresh build.
    35	
    36	Fails on: an unclassified identity-referencing selector, a CLASSIFIED entry that no longer
    37	exists in the ABI, a "blocked" entry missing from the denylist, or a denylist entry not
    38	classified "blocked".

   121	def sig(entry):
   122	    def t(p):
   123	        if p["type"].startswith("tuple"):
   124	            return "(" + ",".join(t(c) for c in p["components"]) + ")" + p["type"][5:]
   125	        return p["type"]
   126	    return f"{entry['name']}({','.join(t(i) for i in entry.get('inputs', []))})"

   128	
   129	def blocks(text):
   130	    """name -> list of (params text, modifiers text, body text) for functions and modifiers."""
   131	    out = {}
   132	    for m in re.finditer(r"\b(function|modifier)\s+(\w+)\s*\(", text):
   133	        name, i = m.group(2), m.end()
   134	        depth = 1
   135	        while depth:
   136	            depth += {"(": 1, ")": -1}.get(text[i], 0)
   137	            i += 1
   138	        params = text[m.end():i - 1]
   139	        j = i
   140	        while j < len(text) and text[j] not in "{;":
   141	            j += 1
   142	        head = text[i:j]
   143	        body = ""
   144	        if j < len(text) and text[j] == "{":
   145	            depth, k = 1, j + 1
   146	            while depth:
   147	                depth += {"{": 1, "}": -1}.get(text[k], 0)
   148	                k += 1
   149	            body = text[j:k]
   150	        out.setdefault(name, []).append((params, head, body))

   194	    }
