   860	    function _revokeBLSPublicKey(address validator) internal {
   861	        BLSValidatorKey storage existing = _blsKeys[validator];
   862	        if (!existing.isActive) return;
   863	        uint8 slot = existing.index;
   864	        existing.isActive = false;
   865	        validatorAtSlot[slot] = address(0);
   866	        emit BLSPublicKeyRevoked(validator, slot);
   867	    }
   868	
   869	    /// @notice CC-48 round-3 recovery: release a `blsKeyOwner` binding that no ACTIVE
   870	    ///         slot is using.
   871	    /// @dev    The binding is intentionally permanent on the normal paths — clearing it
   872	    ///         on `revokeBLSPublicKey` would let a revoked key be re-claimed by a
   873	    ///         different address, which is exactly the duplicate-key condition the
   874	    ///         binding exists to prevent. This function is the ONE escape hatch for a
   875	    ///         binding created in error, and it is deliberately narrow:
   876	    ///           - owner-only (governance action, not a validator-facing one), and
   877	    ///           - refuses while ANY active slot holds this key, so a live signer's
   878	    ///             binding can never be released out from under it. Combined with the
   879	    ///             now-mandatory PoP on both registration paths, the only way to reach
   880	    ///             this function's precondition is a key that is registered nowhere.
   881	    ///         Runbook: revoke the slot first (`revokeBLSPublicKey`), confirm
   882	    ///         `getBLSPublicKey(...).isActive == false` for every holder, then release.
   883	    ///         After release the key may be re-registered by whoever can produce a PoP
   884	    ///         for it — i.e. its actual holder.
   885	    /// @param  keyHash keccak256(abi.encode(pk.x_a, pk.x_b, pk.y_a, pk.y_b)).
   886	    function releaseKeyBinding(bytes32 keyHash) external onlyOwner {
   887	        address boundTo = blsKeyOwner[keyHash];
   888	        if (boundTo == address(0)) revert InvalidParameter("keyHash");
   889	        // Registration admits this key only for boundTo. Until this binding is
   890	        // released, no other address can hold it, even after revocation or rotation.
   891	        BLSValidatorKey storage k = _blsKeys[boundTo];
   892	        if (k.isActive && keccak256(abi.encode(k.publicKey)) == keyHash) {
   893	            revert KeyBindingStillActive(keyHash, boundTo);
   894	        }
   895	        delete blsKeyOwner[keyHash];
   896	        emit BLSKeyBindingReleased(keyHash, boundTo);
   897	    }
   898	
   899	    /// @notice View accessor returning the stored G1 public key + slot for a validator.
   900	    function getBLSPublicKey(address validator)
   901	        external
   902	        view
   903	        returns (BLS.G1Point memory publicKey, uint8 slot, bool isActive)
   904	    {
   905	        BLSValidatorKey memory k = _blsKeys[validator];
   906	        return (k.publicKey, k.index, k.isActive);
   907	    }
   908	
   909	    /// @notice External BLS pairing verification used by Registry / ReputationSystem.
   910	    /// @dev    P0-1: callers cannot supply pkAgg or msgG2 anymore. Both are
   910	    /// @dev    P0-1: callers cannot supply pkAgg or msgG2 anymore. Both are
   911	    ///         derived deterministically — pkAgg from `signerMask` against the
   912	    ///         on-chain validator set, msgG2 from `expectedMessageHash`. Returns
   913	    ///         true iff the pairing equation holds and at least
   914	    ///         `requiredThreshold` distinct on-chain validators are selected.
   915	    /// @param  expectedMessageHash The exact hash the signers committed to.
   916	    /// @param  signerMask Bitmask of signing validator slots (bit i = slot i+1).
   917	    /// @param  requiredThreshold Caller's minimum signer count requirement.
   918	    /// @param  sigBytes abi.encode(BLS.G2Point) of the aggregated G2 signature.
   919	    function verify(bytes32 expectedMessageHash, uint256 signerMask, uint256 requiredThreshold, bytes calldata sigBytes)
   920	        external
   921	        view
   922	        returns (bool)
   923	    {
   924	        if (signerMask == 0) revert EmptySignerMask();
   925	        if (requiredThreshold < minThreshold) {
   926	            revert InvalidThreshold();
   927	        }
   928	        if (requiredThreshold > MAX_VALIDATORS) {
   929	            revert InvalidThreshold();
   930	        }
   931	
   932	        (BLS.G1Point memory pkAgg, uint256 signerCount) = _reconstructPkAgg(signerMask);
   933	        if (signerCount < requiredThreshold) {
   934	            revert InvalidSignatureCount(signerCount, requiredThreshold);
   935	        }
   936	
   937	        BLS.G2Point memory sig = abi.decode(sigBytes, (BLS.G2Point));
   938	        BLS.G2Point memory msgG2 = BLS.hashToG2(abi.encodePacked(expectedMessageHash));
   939	
   940	        BLS.G1Point[] memory g1s = new BLS.G1Point[](2);
   941	        BLS.G2Point[] memory g2s = new BLS.G2Point[](2);
   942	        g1s[0] = _getG1Generator();
   943	        g2s[0] = sig;
   944	        g1s[1] = _negateG1Point(pkAgg);
   945	        g2s[1] = msgG2;
   946	
   947	        return BLS.pairing(g1s, g2s);
   948	    }
   949	
   950	    /// @notice Step 1 of the two-step slash: a DVT quorum pre-flags `operator` for
   951	    ///         slashing (SP.queueSlash), blocking their withdraw until the slash is
   952	    ///         executed (verifyAndExecute) or the owner cancels it. Kept SEPARATE
   953	    ///         from execution so the flag lands in an earlier tx — closing the
   954	    ///         front-run window an atomic queue+execute would reopen.
   955	    /// @dev    Dedicated (not the generic executeProposal) so it (a) is gated at the
   956	    ///         per-severity slash threshold, (b) does NOT consume a proposalId /
   957	    ///         executedProposals slot, leaving the execute-step proposalId intact (a
   958	    ///         BLS re-queue is NOT a no-op on SP: it refreshes slashQueues provenance and
   959	    ///         timestamp, emits again, and may revert during the BLS cooldown), and (c) requires a
   960	    ///         real quorum, so no single validator can DoS an operator's withdraw.
   961	    ///         The queue message is domain-separated ("QUEUE_SLASH") so a queue proof
   962	    ///         can never be replayed as an execute proof.
   963	    ///         Accepted residual: unused pre-rotation queue proofs remain valid. The
   964	    ///         pre-flag moves no funds and is always owner-cancellable via
   965	    ///         SuperPaymasterAdmin.cancelSlash. A BLS-queued freeze has no time expiry
   966	    ///         in SuperPaymasterStorage._isSlashPending; GUARDIAN_SLASH_FREEZE expires
   967	    ///         only guardian queues. usedSlashQueueHashes makes each (operator,
   968	    ///         level, epoch) one-shot. BLS fund movement requires a separate slash
   969	    ///         proof; execution through the current DVT requires an id at or above
   970	    ///         proposalFloor. Owner-direct execution with caller-chosen ids is exempt.
   971	    function queueSlashWithConsensus(address operator, uint8 slashLevel, uint256 epoch, bytes calldata proof)
   972	        external
   973	        nonReentrant
   974	    {
   975	        if (msg.sender != DVT_VALIDATOR && msg.sender != owner()) {
   976	            revert UnauthorizedCaller(msg.sender);
   977	        }
   978	        if (operator == address(0)) revert InvalidTarget(operator);
   979	        if (slashLevel > uint8(ISuperPaymasterSlash.SlashLevel.MAJOR)) revert InvalidParameter("slashLevel");
   980	        uint256 requiredThreshold = slashThresholds[slashLevel];
   981	        bytes32 expectedMessageHash =
   982	            keccak256(abi.encode(domainSeparator(), TAG_QUEUE_SLASH, operator, slashLevel, epoch));
   983	        // Replay guard: a consumed queue proof cannot re-flag the operator after a
   984	        // cancel/execute cleared the flag. A legitimate re-queue uses a new epoch.
   985	        if (usedSlashQueueHashes[expectedMessageHash]) revert SlashQueueProofAlreadyUsed(expectedMessageHash);
   986	        _checkSignatures(proof, expectedMessageHash, requiredThreshold);
   987	        uint256 signerMask = abi.decode(proof[:32], (uint256));
   988	        _commitSigners(uint256(expectedMessageHash), signerMask, expectedMessageHash);
   989	        usedSlashQueueHashes[expectedMessageHash] = true;
   990	        ISuperPaymasterSlash(SUPERPAYMASTER).queueSlash(operator);
   991	        emit SlashPreQueued(operator, slashLevel, epoch, requiredThreshold);
   992	    }
   993	
   994	    /// @notice Execute slash or reputation consensus with a floor on the current DVT path.
   995	    /// @dev Rotation invalidates earlier DVT allocations on both branches, even if
   996	    ///      their allocating DVT becomes current again. Owner-direct execution with
   997	    ///      caller-chosen ids is the documented owner-mediated residual. All callers
   998	    ///      still require nonzero ids below 2^128. Direct Registry reputation proofs
   999	    ///      retain their source gate, per-user epoch and +/-100 score-delta bounds.
  1000	    function verifyAndExecute(
  1001	        uint256 proposalId,
  1002	        address operator,
  1003	        uint8 slashLevel,
  1004	        address[] calldata repUsers,
  1005	        uint256[] calldata newScores,
  1006	        uint256 epoch,
  1007	        bytes32 evidenceHash,
  1008	        bytes calldata proof
  1009	    ) external nonReentrant {
  1010	        if (msg.sender != DVT_VALIDATOR && msg.sender != owner()) {
  1011	            revert UnauthorizedCaller(msg.sender);
  1012	        }
  1013	        // Raw ids (slash AND reputation) live below 2^128, providing 128-bit
  1014	        // cryptographic separation from generic and queue keccak keys. Only the DVT
  1015	        // path uses DVT-allocated ids; owner-direct ids are caller-chosen.
  1016	        if (proposalId == 0 || proposalId >> 128 != 0 || (msg.sender == DVT_VALIDATOR && proposalId < proposalFloor)) {
  1017	            revert InvalidProposalId();
  1018	        }
  1019	        if (executedProposals[proposalId]) {
  1020	            revert ProposalAlreadyExecuted(proposalId);
  1021	        }
  1022	        // CC-48 round-2: reject the combined shape before any signature work. See
  1023	        // CombinedProposalNotSupported — the aggregator and Registry pre-images for a
  1024	        // reputation batch can only agree when operator/slashLevel are absent, so a
  1025	        // reputation proposal carrying a slash target was never executable anyway
  1026	        // (it reverted deep inside Registry with an opaque BLSFailed). Failing here,
  1027	        // by name, turns a silent dead path into an explicit protocol rule.
  1028	        if (repUsers.length != 0 && (operator != address(0) || slashLevel != 0)) {
  1029	            revert CombinedProposalNotSupported();
  1030	        }
  1031	        // CC-48 round-3 LOW: pin the slash-only shape from the OTHER side too. The
  1032	        // slash pre-image commits to (proposalId, operator, slashLevel, epoch,
  1033	        // evidenceHash) and NOT to `newScores`, so a non-empty `newScores` here would be
  1034	        // caller-controlled input that passed a signature check without being covered by
  1035	        // any signature. It is unused on this branch today; requiring it empty means it
  1036	        // can never become used-but-unsigned by a later edit.
  1037	        if (repUsers.length == 0) {
  1038	            if (newScores.length != 0) revert CombinedProposalNotSupported();
  1039	            // A proposal with no batch, no target and no severity only burns a
  1040	            // proposalId in this contract and in Registry. It needs a real quorum, so it
  1041	            // is not an attack surface — but a no-op shape on a consensus path is a
  1042	            // place for future divergence, and CC-48 is about making the protocol rules
  1043	            // explicit rather than incidental.
  1044	            if (operator == address(0) && slashLevel == 0) revert EmptyProposalNotSupported();
  1045	        }
  1046	
  1047	        // 1. Construct expected message + resolve the required threshold.
  1048	        // The signed message MUST commit to chainid to prevent cross-chain replay.
  1049	        bytes32 expectedMessageHash;
  1050	        uint256 requiredThreshold;
  1051	        if (repUsers.length == 0) {
  1052	            // Slash-only consensus path: bar = per-severity governance table
  1053	            // (2/3/3 at bootstrap), and the signed message additionally commits to
  1054	            // `evidenceHash` so every slash is bound on-chain to the off-chain
  1055	            // evidence that justified it. Keeps the reputation branch (below)
  1056	            // byte-identical so Registry's independent re-verification still matches.
  1057	            if (slashLevel > uint8(ISuperPaymasterSlash.SlashLevel.MAJOR)) revert InvalidParameter("slashLevel");
  1058	            requiredThreshold = slashThresholds[slashLevel];
  1059	            expectedMessageHash = keccak256(
  1060	                abi.encode(domainSeparator(), TAG_EXECUTE_SLASH, proposalId, operator, slashLevel, epoch, evidenceHash)
  1061	            );
  1062	        } else {
  1063	            // Reputation-only path: six top-level fields +
  1064	            // defaultThreshold, so Registry.batchUpdateGlobalReputation's
  1065	            // re-verification reconstructs the identical hash.
  1066	            requiredThreshold = defaultThreshold;
  1067	            // Byte-identical to Registry._reputationMessageHash. operator/slashLevel
  1068	            // are NOT fields here: they are pinned to zero by the combined-shape guard
  1069	            // above, so including them could only re-introduce the divergence.
  1070	            expectedMessageHash = _reputationMessageHash(proposalId, repUsers, newScores, epoch);
  1071	        }
  1072	
  1073	        // 2. Verify BLS pairing using on-chain reconstructed pkAgg (P0-1).
  1074	        _checkSignatures(proof, expectedMessageHash, requiredThreshold);
  1075	
  1076	        // 2b. A' attribution snapshot (CC-89 stage-2): commit to the signer ADDRESS
  1077	        //     set NOW — after signatures verified, before any revoke can reassign a
  1078	        //     slot — so a later fraud proof can attribute this proposal to the real
  1079	        //     addresses that signed it. Covers BOTH branches (slash-only + rep).
  1080	        uint256 signerMask = abi.decode(proof[:32], (uint256));
  1081	        _commitSigners(proposalId, signerMask, expectedMessageHash);
  1082	
  1083	        // 3. Update Global Reputation in Registry
  1084	        if (repUsers.length > 0) {
  1085	            REGISTRY.batchUpdateGlobalReputation(proposalId, repUsers, newScores, epoch, proof);
  1086	            emit ReputationEpochTriggered(epoch, repUsers.length);
  1087	        } else {
  1088	            // Slash-only proposal: mark proposalId in Registry to prevent cross-path replay
  1089	            // (attacker holding valid proof cannot reuse proposalId via direct Registry call)
  1090	            REGISTRY.markProposalExecuted(proposalId);
  1091	            emit SlashConsensusReached(proposalId, slashLevel, requiredThreshold, evidenceHash);
  1092	        }
  1093	
  1094	        // 4. Execute Slash if operator is provided
  1095	        if (operator != address(0)) {
  1096	            _executeSlash(proposalId, operator, slashLevel, proof);
  1097	        }
  1098	
  1099	        executedProposals[proposalId] = true;
  1100	        if (DVT_VALIDATOR != address(0)) {
  1101	            IDVTValidator(DVT_VALIDATOR).markProposalExecuted(proposalId);
  1102	        }
  1103	    }
  1104	
  1105	    /**
  1106	     * @notice Execute any proposal via BLS consensus (Generic DVT)
  1107	     * @dev Allows executing arbitrary calls to authorized target contracts after BLS signature verification.
  1108	     *      The target contract is responsible for its own access control (checking msg.sender == BLSAggregator).
  1109	     *      Accepted residual: unused pre-rotation generic proofs remain owner-executable;
  1110	     *      proposalFloor applies only to slash and aggregator-routed reputation executed through the current DVT.
  1260	    /// @dev Reconstruct the aggregate public key from `signerMask` by accumulating
  1261	    ///      every selected validator's stored G1 point with the EIP-2537 G1ADD
  1262	    ///      precompile. Reverts if any selected slot is empty/inactive — this is
  1263	    ///      the gate that closes P0-1: the caller cannot inject an unrelated
  1264	    ///      pkAgg, every contributing key is read straight from on-chain state.
  1265	    ///
  1266	    ///      P0 follow-up — every selected slot is also re-validated in real time:
  1267	    ///        1. `BLSValidatorKey.isActive` must be true (key not revoked).
  1268	    ///        2. The slot's validator must still hold ROLE_DVT in the Registry.
  1269	    ///        3. The slot's validator's locked GToken stake under ROLE_DVT must
  1270	    ///           be >= the role's `minStake` from `Registry.getRoleConfig`.
  1271	    ///      Any one failure reverts the entire aggregation. This closes the
  1272	    ///      attack where a registered validator exits / unstakes / loses their
  1273	    ///      role but keeps voting power because the slot pointer was never
  1274	    ///      cleared. Aggregator owns the trust decision (Registry + Staking) so
  1275	    ///      no callback into DVTValidator is needed — avoids a circular
  1276	    ///      BLSAggregator ↔ DVTValidator dependency.
  1277	    function _reconstructPkAgg(uint256 signerMask) internal view returns (BLS.G1Point memory pkAgg, uint256 count) {
  1278	        // Reject mask bits beyond MAX_VALIDATORS to prevent silent truncation —
  1279	        // a clever attacker could otherwise pad with high-order bits hoping the
  1280	        // contract ignored them.
  1281	        if (signerMask >> uint256(MAX_VALIDATORS) != 0) {
  1282	            revert SlotOutOfRange(uint8(MAX_VALIDATORS + 1));
  1283	        }
  1284	
  1285	        // Resolve role+stake context once for the whole loop. The Registry
  1286	        // pointer is immutable; the staking pointer (and minStake) are read
  1287	        // from Registry per-call so governance can rotate either without
  1288	        // redeploying BLSAggregator.
  1289	        bytes32 roleDvt = keccak256("DVT");
  1290	        IGTokenStaking staking = IRegistryStakingAwareBLS(address(REGISTRY)).GTOKEN_STAKING();
  1291	        if (address(staking) == address(0)) revert StakingNotConfigured();
  1292	        uint256 minStake = REGISTRY.getRoleConfig(roleDvt).minStake;
  1293	
  1294	        bool initialized = false;
  1295	        for (uint8 slot = 1; slot <= MAX_VALIDATORS; slot++) {
  1296	            if ((signerMask >> uint256(slot - 1)) & 1 == 0) continue;
  1297	
  1298	            address v = validatorAtSlot[slot];
  1299	            if (v == address(0)) revert UnknownValidatorSlot(slot);
  1300	            BLSValidatorKey storage k = _blsKeys[v];
  1301	            if (!k.isActive) revert UnknownValidatorSlot(slot);
  1302	            if (suspended[v]) revert ValidatorSuspended(v);
  1303	
  1304	            // Real-time liveness — cheap on-chain reads, but they catch every
  1305	            // post-registration drift the original P0-1 fix missed.
  1306	            if (!REGISTRY.hasRole(roleDvt, v)) {
  1307	                revert SlotValidatorRoleRevoked(slot, v);
  1308	            }
  1309	            // CC-48 BLOCKER-1: an exit NOTICE must not take effect in the block it is
  1310	            // filed. Pre-fix, `readyAt != 0` alone disqualified the slot, so any single
  1311	            // ROLE_DVT member could watch the mempool, front-run a quorum transaction
  1312	            // with requestGuardianExit() (~1 SSTORE), make it revert, then immediately
  1313	            // cancelGuardianExit() — a free, repeatable, self-erasing 1-of-N halt on
  1314	            // every BLS-gated governance path. Honouring the notice only once
  1315	            // `block.timestamp >= readyAt` means the exclusion is always announced
  1316	            // GUARDIAN_EXIT_DELAY in advance: it cannot touch an in-flight proof, and
  1317	            // governance has the whole notice period to seat a replacement.
  1318	            // Note the exclusion is deliberately NOT bounded by `expiresAt`: a lapsed notice
  1319	            // keeps excluding the slot, and cancelling (at the cost of a cooldown) is the only
  1320	            // way back in. Letting an expired notice silently re-admit a guardian would be the
  1321	            // looser of the two behaviours, and this gate errs toward exclusion —
  1322	            // see test_ExpiredNoticeIsClearedByCancel.
  1323	            uint64 exitReadyAt = guardianExitRequests[v].readyAt;
  1324	            if (exitReadyAt != 0 && block.timestamp >= uint256(exitReadyAt)) {
  1325	                revert SlotValidatorExitPending(slot, v);
  1326	            }
  1327	            (uint128 amount,,,,) = staking.roleLocks(v, roleDvt);
  1328	            if (uint256(amount) < minStake) {
  1329	                revert SlotValidatorStakeBelowMinimum(slot, v, uint256(amount), minStake);
  1330	            }
  1331	
  1332	            if (!initialized) {
  1333	                pkAgg = k.publicKey;
  1334	                initialized = true;
  1335	            } else {
  1336	                pkAgg = BLS.add(pkAgg, k.publicKey);
  1337	            }
  1338	            count += 1;
  1339	        }
  1340	
  1341	        if (!initialized) revert EmptySignerMask();
  1342	    }
  1343	
  1344	    /// @notice A' attribution helper (CC-89 stage-2): compute the signer-set
  1345	    ///         commitment for a proposal. Deliberately re-walks signerMask instead
  1346	    ///         of threading signers out of _reconstructPkAgg, to keep the
  1347	    ///         load-bearing verification path untouched; N ≤ MAX_VALIDATORS so the
  1348	    ///         extra SLOAD loop + insertion sort are negligible vs the BLS pairing.
  1349	    /// @dev    MUST be called only AFTER _checkSignatures has passed — that guarantees
  1350	    ///         signerMask has no out-of-range bits and every selected slot resolves
  1351	    ///         to a live validator (validatorAtSlot != 0). Canonical order = ascending
  1352	    ///         uint160(address) (no duplicates: each slot maps to one address), so an
  1353	    ///         off-chain verifier sorting claimedSigners the same way reproduces the
  1354	    ///         identical commitment. signerMask is bound in too (slot layout), and the
  1355	    ///         encoding is domain-separated to prevent cross-proposal/chain/contract reuse.
  1356	    function _computeSignersCommitment(uint256 signerMask, uint256 key, bytes32 messageHash)
  1357	        internal
  1358	        view
  1359	        returns (bytes32)
  1360	    {
  1361	        // popcount → exact array size.
  1362	        uint256 n;
  1363	        {
  1364	            uint256 m = signerMask;
  1365	            while (m != 0) {
  1366	                m &= (m - 1);
  1367	                n++;
  1368	            }
  1369	        }
  1370	
  1371	        address[] memory signers = new address[](n);
  1372	        uint256 k;
  1373	        for (uint8 slot = 1; slot <= MAX_VALIDATORS; slot++) {
  1374	            if ((signerMask >> uint256(slot - 1)) & 1 == 0) continue;
  1375	            signers[k++] = validatorAtSlot[slot];
  1376	        }
  1377	
  1378	        // Insertion sort ascending by uint160(address) (n ≤ MAX_VALIDATORS).
  1379	        for (uint256 i = 1; i < n; i++) {
  1380	            address sortKey = signers[i];
  1381	            uint256 j = i;
  1382	            while (j > 0 && uint160(signers[j - 1]) > uint160(sortKey)) {
  1383	                signers[j] = signers[j - 1];
  1384	                unchecked {
  1385	                    j--;
  1386	                }
  1387	            }
  1388	            signers[j] = sortKey;
  1389	        }
  1390	
  1391	        return keccak256(
  1392	            abi.encode(
  1393	                domainSeparator(),
  1394	                TAG_SIGNERS_COMMITMENT,
  1395	                key,
  1396	                messageHash,
  1397	                signerMask,
  1398	                signers,
  1399	                blockhash(block.number - 1)
  1400	            )
  1401	        );
  1402	    }
  1403	
  1404	    function _commitSigners(uint256 key, uint256 signerMask, bytes32 messageHash) internal {
  1405	        proposalSignersCommitment[key] = _computeSignersCommitment(signerMask, key, messageHash);
  1406	        emit SignersCommitted(key, signerMask);
  1407	    }
  1408	
  1409	    /// @notice Canonical reputation-batch pre-image. MUST stay byte-identical to
  1410	    ///         `Registry._reputationMessageHash` — Registry independently re-verifies
  1411	    ///         the same signature, and any divergence turns every reputation proposal
  1412	    ///         into an unexplained `BLSFailed`.
  1413	    /// @dev    Public so DVT/SDK can reproduce it without re-deriving the layout.
  1414	    function reputationMessageHash(
  1415	        uint256 proposalId,
  1416	        address[] calldata users,
  1417	        uint256[] calldata newScores,
  1418	        uint256 epoch
  1419	    ) external view returns (bytes32) {
  1420	        return _reputationMessageHash(proposalId, users, newScores, epoch);
  1421	    }
  1422	
  1423	    function _reputationMessageHash(
  1424	        uint256 proposalId,
  1425	        address[] calldata users,
  1426	        uint256[] calldata newScores,
  1427	        uint256 epoch
  1428	    ) internal view returns (bytes32) {
  1429	        return keccak256(abi.encode(domainSeparator(), TAG_REPUTATION, proposalId, users, newScores, epoch));
  1430	    }
  1431	
  1432	    function _checkSignatures(bytes calldata proof, bytes32 expectedMessageHash, uint256 requiredThreshold)
  1433	        internal
  1434	        view
  1435	    {
  1436	        // P0-1: proof = abi.encode(uint256 signerMask, bytes sigG2). pkG1 and
  1437	        // msgG2 are NEVER read from the proof — they're reconstructed/derived
  1438	        // on-chain so a forged proof cannot satisfy the pairing.
  1439	        (uint256 signerMask, bytes memory sigG2Bytes) = abi.decode(proof, (uint256, bytes));
  1440	
  1441	        // Absolute floor for ALL paths. The generic executeProposal path enforces
  1442	        // the stricter `minThreshold` before it reaches here (see executeProposal),
  1443	        // so this hard floor lets the slash path verify a 2-of-3 WARNING without
  1444	        // widening the generic path's 3-of-N minimum.
  1445	        if (requiredThreshold < SLASH_THRESHOLD_FLOOR) revert InvalidThreshold();
  1446	        if (requiredThreshold > MAX_VALIDATORS) revert InvalidThreshold();
  1447	
  1448	        (BLS.G1Point memory pkAgg, uint256 count) = _reconstructPkAgg(signerMask);
  1449	        if (count < requiredThreshold) revert InvalidSignatureCount(count, requiredThreshold);
  1450	
  1451	        BLS.G2Point memory sig = abi.decode(sigG2Bytes, (BLS.G2Point));
  1452	        BLS.G2Point memory msgG2 = BLS.hashToG2(abi.encodePacked(expectedMessageHash));
  1453	
  1454	        BLS.G1Point[] memory g1s = new BLS.G1Point[](2);
  1455	        BLS.G2Point[] memory g2s = new BLS.G2Point[](2);
  1456	        g1s[0] = _getG1Generator();
  1457	        g2s[0] = sig;
  1458	        g1s[1] = _negateG1Point(pkAgg);
  1459	        g2s[1] = msgG2;
  1460	
  1520	
  1521	    function _executeSlash(uint256 proposalId, address operator, uint8 level, bytes calldata proof) internal {
  1522	        ISuperPaymasterSlash.SlashLevel sLevel = ISuperPaymasterSlash.SlashLevel(level);
  1523	        // Two-step slash: the operator MUST already be flagged via SP.queueSlash in a
  1524	        // SEPARATE, earlier consensus tx — the dedicated queueSlashWithConsensus entry
  1525	        // (queueSlash IS selector-blocked on the generic executeProposal path). That
  1526	        // flag blocks the operator's withdraw() between the queue and this execute.
  1527	        // Queuing + slashing atomically here would REOPEN the front-run window (the
  1528	        // operator could drain their balance before the slash lands), regressing the
  1529	        // HIGH-1 two-step protection — so the flag stays a distinct prior step. Only
  1530	        // the fund-moving executeSlashWithBLS runs here, and it is reachable solely
  1531	        // through this per-severity threshold-checked path.
  1532	        ISuperPaymasterSlash(SUPERPAYMASTER).executeSlashWithBLS(operator, sLevel, proof);
  1533	        emit SlashExecuted(proposalId, operator, level);
  1534	    }
  1535

  1537	    // Admin Functions
  1538	    // ====================================
  1539	
  1540	    function setSuperPaymaster(address _sp) external onlyOwner {
  1541	        if (_sp == address(0)) revert InvalidParameter("Zero address");
  1542	        emit SuperPaymasterUpdated(SUPERPAYMASTER, _sp);
  1543	        SUPERPAYMASTER = _sp;
  1544	    }
  1545	
  1546	    /// @notice Atomically capture all outgoing allocations before activating a DVT.
  1547	    /// @dev Reverts on an unreadable outgoing counter; never guesses a replacement floor.
  1548	    ///      Raising the floor invalidates earlier slash and aggregator-routed reputation
  1549	    ///      proposals executed through the current DVT, including after reactivation.
  1550	    function setDVTValidator(address _dv) external onlyOwner {
  1551	        uint256 floor = DVT_VALIDATOR == address(0) ? proposalFloor : IDVTValidator(DVT_VALIDATOR).nextProposalId();
  1552	        _setDVTValidator(_dv, floor);
  1553	    }
  1554	
  1555	    /// @notice Emergency rotation when the outgoing counter cannot be read.
  1556	    /// @dev Owner must audit all outgoing allocations and supply their exclusive upper
  1557	    ///      bound. No on-chain read can recover unavailable history. A bad owner floor
  1558	    ///      can re-admit unused authorizations; this is explicit governance authority.
  1559	    ///      The stored floor can never decrease. 2^128 preserves exhausted namespaces.
  1560	    function setDVTValidatorWithFloor(address _dv, uint256 floor) external onlyOwner {
  1561	        if (floor < proposalFloor) revert InvalidProposalFloor();
  1562	        _setDVTValidator(_dv, floor);
  1563	    }
  1564	
  1565	    function _setDVTValidator(address _dv, uint256 floor) internal {
  1566	        if (floor > uint256(1) << 128) revert InvalidProposalFloor();
  1567	        if (IDVTValidator(_dv).BLS_AGGREGATOR() != address(this)
  1568	            || IDVTValidator(_dv).REGISTRY() != address(REGISTRY)) revert InvalidDVTPairing();
  1569	        if (floor > proposalFloor) proposalFloor = floor;
  1570	        emit DVTValidatorUpdated(DVT_VALIDATOR, _dv);
  1571	        DVT_VALIDATOR = _dv;
  1572	    }
  1573	
  1574	    /**
  1575	     * @notice Set the generic/verify minimum; slash consensus has its own severity floors
  1576	     */
  1577	    function setMinThreshold(uint256 _newThreshold) external onlyOwner {
  1578	        if (_newThreshold < 2) revert InvalidThreshold();
  1579	        if (_newThreshold > MAX_VALIDATORS) revert InvalidThreshold();
  1580	        // Invariant: minThreshold must not exceed defaultThreshold
  1581	        if (_newThreshold > defaultThreshold) revert InvalidThreshold();
  1582	        emit ThresholdUpdated(minThreshold, _newThreshold);
  1583	        minThreshold = _newThreshold;
  1584	    }
  1585	
  1586	    /// @notice H-02: toggle permissionless (stake + proof-of-possession) self-registration
  1587	    ///         of BLS validator keys. Default off — flip on once governance is ready to let
  1588	    ///         staked ROLE_DVT validators onboard their own keys without an owner call.
  1589	    function setPermissionlessBLSRegistration(bool enabled) external onlyOwner {
  1590	        permissionlessBLSRegistration = enabled;
  1591	        emit PermissionlessBLSRegistrationSet(enabled);
  1592	    }
  1593	
  1594	    /**
  1595	     * @notice Set the reputation-branch threshold in verifyAndExecute
  1596	     */
  1597	    function setDefaultThreshold(uint256 _newThreshold) external onlyOwner {
  1598	        if (_newThreshold < minThreshold) revert InvalidThreshold();
  1599	        if (_newThreshold > MAX_VALIDATORS) revert InvalidThreshold();
  1600	        emit ThresholdUpdated(defaultThreshold, _newThreshold);
  1601	        defaultThreshold = _newThreshold;
  1602	    }

  1604	    /// @notice Propose wiring/rotating the guardian-collusion fraud-proof verifier
  1605	    ///         (Protocol B stage 2 detection layer, supplied by the DVT repo).
  1606	    ///         Owner-gated, and matured by `applyFraudProofVerifier` only after
  1607	    ///         `VERIFIER_ROTATION_DELAY`.
  1608	    /// @dev    This is the SOLE authorization surface for an unbounded, permissionless,
  1609	    ///         configurable-fraction slash path, so CC-48 MEDIUM-1 moved it to a two-step,
  1610	    ///         delay-guarded rotation: propose -> wait VERIFIER_ROTATION_DELAY -> apply.
  1611	    ///         `owner` MUST be a Safe-COMPATIBLE M-of-N owner. For THIS function a
  1612	    ///         TimelockController in front of it is genuine defence in depth (arming is
  1613	    ///         delayed anyway, so an extra delay costs nothing); for
  1614	    ///         `emergencyDisarmFraudProofVerifier` it is not, because a timelocked
  1615	    ///         emergency stop is not an emergency stop — see the residual-risk paragraph

codex
The source confirms the generic executor is intentionally broad: the safety boundary is a six-selector denylist plus each target’s own authorization. I’m checking the classification table against the actual privilege predicates rather than relying on the script’s textual scan, and I’m treating owner-direct queue/execution and direct Registry reputation as separate ingress paths because they have materially different floor/evidence semantics.

 succeeded in 10ms:
   181	### DVT proposal-id rotation
   182	
   183	`DVTValidator-0.8.0` takes `(registry, firstProposalId)`; there is no predecessor
   184	argument or state. `firstProposalId` is nonzero and below `2^128`. The retained
   185	BLSAggregator-4.14.0 owns a monotonic `proposalFloor`. Activation atomically
   186	captures the outgoing DVT's next allocation, including pending IDs, and validates
   187	the successor's aggregator and Registry pointers. Every allocation reads that
   188	floor once. Empty intermediates and A→B→A rollback cannot reuse allocated IDs.
   189	The ordinary setter fails closed if the outgoing counter cannot be read. The
   190	explicit-floor recovery operation requires governance to supply an audited upper
   191	bound on all allocated IDs; it must never lower the stored floor. This recovery
   192	is an immediate owner authority, not automatic discovery of missing history.
   193	
   194	Pending slash and aggregator-routed reputation proposals executed through the
   195	current DVT need fresh IDs and proofs after rotation, including after reactivation.
   196	The floor does not revoke owner-direct `verifyAndExecute` with caller-chosen IDs,
   197	generic proofs, authorized direct Registry reputation proofs or unused consensus
   198	queue pre-flags; see the disclosed M1 residuals below. Full-width hashed
   199	ReputationSystem IDs remain valid on the direct Registry route.
   200	
   201	### Validator-removal quorum floor
   202	
   203	The survival check protects each currently reachable configured threshold:
   204	`minThreshold`, `defaultThreshold`, and every severity threshold. With one
   205	eligible signer leaving, crossing a threshold is exactly equality with the
   206	pre-exit committee count. Higher unreachable thresholds do not hide lower working
   207	ones; caller-selected generic thresholds above `minThreshold` are not standing
   208	configured quorums. At an exactly reachable N-of-N quorum, seat a replacement or
   209	change policy before voluntary exit or DVT removal. Direct owner revocation and
   210	suspension are explicit emergency overrides.
   211	
   212	Suspension is authoritative on the retained aggregator and is checked both during
   213	registration and while reconstructing every aggregate key. It survives rotation
   214	and rollback and can be set even without local admission or an active key. Use
   215	owner `revokeAndSuspend` for atomic emergency eviction. Reinstatement requires
   216	explicit owner clearance on the aggregator; DVT admission alone cannot clear it.
   217	
   218	The DVT and aggregator owners must remain the same Safe-compatible M-of-N owner.
   219	Rotation delegates key-registration authority as well as proposal forwarding;
   220	it is therefore more than the owner's existing direct execution permission.
   221	Verifier rotation separately retains its contract-enforced five-day delay.
   222	
   223	### Reputation rule disablement
   224	
   225	`Reputation-0.6.0` records rule existence and active state separately. A configured
   772	## Reviewed redesign evidence (2026-11-13)
   773	
   774	The earlier sizes, source hashes, generated-bundle statements and whole-tree test
   775	counts above are historical and superseded by `.rv-redesign.md`. Current versions
   776	are BLSAggregator-4.14.0, DVTValidator-0.8.0 and Reputation-0.6.0. Genuine crypto
   777	tests, including the M1 replay regression, intentionally skip on Cancun; Prague
   778	evidence must execute the mandatory real-precompile sentinel successfully. CC115
   779	tests using AnchoredOverIssueFraudProofVerifier are **local adapter** evidence,
   780	not evidence of compatibility with the unadapted pinned upstream verifier.
   781	The historical Python executor remains byte-identical to origin/main; rc.8
   782	routing belongs in another PR.
   783	
   784	### M1 execution floor and disclosed residuals (2026-11-13)
   785	
   786	`BLSAggregator.verifyAndExecute` rejects `proposalId < proposalFloor` with
   787	`InvalidProposalId` only when `msg.sender == DVT_VALIDATOR`. M1 covers slash and
   788	aggregator-routed reputation proposals executed through the current DVT, where
   789	IDs are DVT-allocated. An allocated id with a ready proof becomes stale on that
   790	path when a later rotation raises the floor; reactivating its original DVT does
   791	not restore it. A newly allocated id at or above the fresh floor remains
   792	executable with its own valid proof. Every caller, including the owner, still
   793	receives `InvalidProposalId` for zero or an id at or above `2^128`. The monotonic
   794	floor preserves the exhausted value `2^128` across rotations.
   795	
   796	The accepted, disclosed residuals are:
   797	
   798	- Owner-direct `verifyAndExecute` accepts caller-chosen ids below the floor.
   799	  This is the documented owner-mediated residual: rotation does not revoke that
   800	  route's unused signed authorizations. Signature, replay and DVT callback checks
   801	  still apply; the floor must not treat an owner-chosen id as a DVT allocation.
   802	- Unused pre-rotation generic proposals remain owner/DVT-executable through
   803	  `executeProposal`; their typed replay keys are separate from the raw-id floor.
   804	- Pre-rotation reputation proofs can still reach Registry through an authorized
   805	  reputation source. That route remains source-gated, enforces per-user epoch
   806	  progression and bounds score changes to ±100. It does not inherit the
   807	  aggregator's execution floor.
   808	- An unused pre-rotation consensus queue proof can still set a reversible
   809	  pre-flag. A BLS-queued freeze has **no time expiry**:
   810	  `SuperPaymasterStorage._isSlashPending` expires only a guardian queue after
   811	  `GUARDIAN_SLASH_FREEZE`. The owner can always cancel the flag with
   812	  `SuperPaymasterAdmin.cancelSlash`. `usedSlashQueueHashes` makes the proof
   813	  one-shot per `(operator, level, epoch)`, even after cancellation. The pre-flag
   814	  moves no funds: execution through the current DVT requires a separate fresh
   815	  slash proof with an id at or above the current floor. Relabelling an old proof
   816	  with a fresh id also fails signature verification. Owner-direct execution with
   817	  caller-chosen ids remains the residual described above. The independent owner
   818	  slash authority is unchanged.
   819	
   820	`RotationFloorExecution.t.sol` covers stale current-DVT execution after reactivation,
   821	fresh execution, later rotation invalidation, stale queue versus DVT fund-moving execution,
   822	exact namespace exhaustion, suspended key ownership and the disclosed queue
   823	residual. Signature-dependent tests execute with genuine Prague precompiles and
   824	skip on Cancun. `test_RotationAllocationFloorPreservesAuthorizedRegistryProof`
   825	also checks that the same stale reputation proof fails through the reactivated DVT
   826	before succeeding through the authorized Registry source. The same real-Registry
   827	suite adds `test_PostRotationDvtReputationUpdateExecutes` and
   828	`test_OwnerDirectLowIdStillExecutesAfterRotation`, proving successful reputation
   829	updates with genuine signatures on both authorized routes. Final suite counts,
   830	runtime size and changed files are recorded in `.rv-narrow.md`; ABI/docs
   831	regeneration is left to the calling session.
   832	
   833	## Generic-path selector gaps and the staking slasher role (2026-11-15)
   834	
   835	BLSAggregator-4.14.0 as merged in #630 had two gaps on the GENERIC `executeProposal` path
   836	(caller-chosen threshold >= `minThreshold`, no `proposalFloor`). Both are closed here.
   837	
   838	1. **`Registry.batchUpdateGlobalReputation` was not blocked.** Registry re-verifies the proof
   839	   itself, but under `blsDomainSeparator() = keccak(DOMAIN_NAME, chainid, blsAggregator, Registry)`.
   840	   That domain does not change on a DVT rotation, and Registry has no floor. A reputation proof
   841	   signed before a rotation and never executed could therefore still execute through a generic call,
   842	   bypassing the M1 floor guarantee.
   843	   - The #630 review recorded this path only as "consumes a raw id". The floor bypass was found
   844	     afterwards.
   845	   - Test: `test_PreRotationReputationProofCannotExecuteThroughGenericPath` (Prague, genuine keys).
   846	     Removing the block turns it red.
   847	2. **`GTokenStaking.slash` and `slashByDVT` were not blocked.** `DeployLive` made the aggregator an
   848	   authorized slasher, so a `minThreshold` quorum could slash any stake by any amount. This bypassed
   849	   severity bps, cooldown and the floor.
   850	   - The live Sepolia 4.11.0 aggregator has the same gap, with `minThreshold` 2. Only its owner (the
   851	     deployer EOA) can call `executeProposal` there.
   852	
   853	**Fixes**
   854	- The H-1 denylist now also blocks `batchUpdateGlobalReputation`, `slash` and `slashByDVT`.
   855	  BLSAggregator is 24,529 B (47 B headroom). Per-selector blocking is close to full: 5.5.x should
   856	  move to a per-target allowlist, or move the gates into the callees.
   857	- Versions are BLSAggregator-4.14.1 and DVTValidator-0.8.1. The 4.14.0 / 0.8.0 merged in #630
   858	  carries the gaps above.
   859	- `scripts/check_generic_selector_coverage.py` (CI, every PR) enumerates every non-view function in
   860	  the SuperPaymaster, Registry, GTokenStaking and DVTValidator ABIs. Each function whose code
   861	  references an aggregator identity must be classified as blocked or allowed-with-reason.
   862	- **No slasher role in 5.5.0.** The aggregator's only stake slash is `executeGuardianSlash`, which
   863	  cannot be reached while `fraudProofVerifier == 0`. `DeployLive` / `DeployAnvil` no longer grant the
   864	  role and assert it is absent.
   865	  - Enabling guardian slash later grants the role in the same timelock batch as
   866	    `proposeFraudProofVerifier`.
   867	  - Before enabling, see YetAnotherAA-Validator#441: the verifier must recompute the 4.14.0 signers
   868	    commitment, which includes `blockhash(block.number-1)`.
   869	- **Review L1.** Owner-forwarded `DVTValidator.registerBLSPublicKey` now requires
   870	  `aggregator.owner() == msg.sender`, as `removeValidator` already did.
   871	  - Test: `test_RegisterBLSPublicKey_OwnerDivergenceClosesOwnerPath`.
   872	
   873	**Disclosed residuals**
   874	- `Registry.updateOperatorBlacklist` stays generic-callable. A pre-rotation proof for the next unused
   875	  `blacklistNonce` remains valid. Its effect is limited to blocking or unblocking users; it moves no
   876	  funds.
   877	- `SP.updatePriceDVT` stays generic-callable. Each update is limited to +-20% against the SP cache.
   878	  The +-20% Chainlink band applies only while Chainlink is fresh (< 2 h). With a stale feed, cumulative
   879	  drift is bounded only by `[MIN, MAX]_ETH_USD_PRICE`, freshness and increasing `updatedAt`.
   880	- `ReputationSystem.setCommunityReputation` stays generic-callable (#631 review L1). It is open to
   881	  any Registry reputation source, and the aggregator is one on both chains. The current quorum can
   882	  therefore write any community score at `minThreshold`, with no proof.
   883	  - `communityReputations` has no reader in `contracts/src`. The coverage script fails if one is
   884	    added; that is the reopen condition.
   885	  - The SDK exposes the value only as a raw getter, documented as unused by the protocol.
   886	- The generic path can call `transfer` / `approve` on any token the aggregator holds. The aggregator
   887	  must hold no assets; step 13 reads its GToken, aPNTs, xPNTs and ETH balances back as 0.
   888	- Every "onlyOwner; the aggregator is not the owner" allowance holds only while the aggregator owns
   889	  nothing and holds no role. This is enforced by the coverage script's deploy-path ownership check
   890	  and by the step-13 readbacks OWNER-NOT-AGGREGATOR and AGG-NOT-DVT-VALIDATOR.
   530	    // CC-48 round-2: versioned domain separation (mirrors BLSAggregator)
   531	    // ====================================
   532	    //
   533	    // Registry re-derives the separator LOCALLY rather than reading it off the
   534	    // aggregator. The whole point of Registry's second verification is that it does
   535	    // not take the aggregator's word for anything; asking the aggregator to name its
   536	    // own domain would hand that back. Because the separator commits to
   537	    // `address(this)` (Registry) and to the aggregator address Registry has
   538	    // configured, the two only agree when the aggregator's immutable REGISTRY really
   539	    // is this Registry — a mis-wired pair fails closed instead of verifying.
   540	    bytes32 internal constant DOMAIN_NAME = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLSConsensus.v1");
   541	    bytes32 internal constant TAG_REPUTATION = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLS.Reputation.v1");
   542	    bytes32 internal constant TAG_BLACKLIST = keccak256("SuperPaymaster.BLS.Blacklist.v1");
   543	
   544	    /// @notice The BLS domain separator this Registry verifies under.
   545	    /// @dev    MUST equal `BLSAggregator.domainSeparator()` of the configured
   546	    ///         aggregator; asserted in tests and exposed for DVT/SDK pinning.
   547	    function blsDomainSeparator() public view returns (bytes32) {
   548	        return keccak256(abi.encode(DOMAIN_NAME, block.chainid, blsAggregator, address(this)));
   549	    }
   550	
   551	    /// @notice Canonical reputation-batch pre-image; byte-identical to
   552	    ///         `BLSAggregator.reputationMessageHash`.
   553	    function _reputationMessageHash(
   554	        uint256 proposalId,
   555	        address[] calldata users,
   556	        uint256[] calldata newScores,
   557	        uint256 epoch
   558	    ) internal view returns (bytes32) {
   559	        return keccak256(
   560	            abi.encode(blsDomainSeparator(), TAG_REPUTATION, proposalId, users, newScores, epoch)
   561	        );
   562	    }
   563	
   564	    /// @dev Shared BLS decode + threshold check + verify helper.
   565	    function _verifyBLS(bytes calldata proof, bytes32 messageHash) internal {
   566	        (uint256 signerMask, bytes memory sigG2Bytes) = abi.decode(proof, (uint256, bytes));
   567	        address agg = blsAggregator;
   568	        uint256 threshold = IBLSAggregator(agg).defaultThreshold();
   569	        uint256 _m = signerMask; uint256 _bits; while (_m != 0) { _m &= (_m - 1); _bits++; }
   570	        if (_bits < threshold) revert InsufficientConsensus();
   571	        if (!IBLSAggregator(agg).verify(messageHash, signerMask, threshold, sigG2Bytes)) revert BLSFailed();
   572	    }
   573	
   574	    function batchUpdateGlobalReputation(
   575	        uint256 proposalId,
   576	        address[] calldata users,
   577	        uint256[] calldata newScores,
   578	        uint256 epoch,
   579	        bytes calldata proof
   580	    ) external nonReentrant {
   581	        if (!isReputationSource[msg.sender]) revert UnauthorizedSource();
   582	        if (users.length != newScores.length) revert LenMismatch();
   583	        if (users.length > 200) revert BatchTooLarge();
   584	        if (proof.length == 0) revert BLSProofRequired();
   585	        if (blsAggregator == address(0)) revert BLSNotConfigured();
   586	        if (proposalId == 0) revert InvalidProposalId();
   587	        // L-C: epoch=0 would skip every user (epoch <= lastReputationEpoch, default 0)
   588	        // yet still burn the proposalId below — a permanently wasted, un-retryable proposal.
   589	        if (epoch == 0) revert InvalidParam();
   590	        // CC-48 round-9 MEDIUM-HIGH-B3: a freshly upgraded proxy reads zero in the
   591	        // population slots while real, already-promoted users exist on-chain. Deriving
   592	        // exposure from that empty population would under-count the live stock by exactly
   593	        // the pre-upgrade issuance, so the reputation path stays SHUT until governance has
   594	        // seeded the population and declared it complete. Fresh deployments are seeded in
   595	        // `initialize`, where the population provably IS empty.
   596	        if (creditPopulationSeededAt == 0) revert CreditPopulationNotSeeded();
   597	        if (executedProposals[proposalId]) revert ProposalAlreadyExecuted();
   598	
   599	        executedProposals[proposalId] = true;
   600	        _verifyBLS(proof, _reputationMessageHash(proposalId, users, newScores, epoch));
   601	
   602	        uint256 aggregateUplift;
   603	        uint256 aggregateRelease;
   604	        uint256 backfill;
   605	        uint256 marker = creditPopulationEpoch + 1;
   606	        uint256 floorLimit = creditTierConfig[1];
   607	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < users.length; ) {
   608	            address user = users[i];
   609	            if (epoch <= lastReputationEpoch[user]) {
   610	                unchecked { ++i; }
   611	                continue;
   612	            }
   613	            uint256 _old = globalReputation[user]; uint256 _new = newScores[i];
   614	            uint256 clamped = (_new > _old)
   615	                ? ((_new - _old > 100) ? _old + 100 : _new)
   616	                : ((_old > _new && _old - _new > 100) ? _old - 100 : _new);
   617	            uint256 oldLimit = creditTierConfig[_levelForReputation(_old)];
   618	            uint256 newLimit = creditTierConfig[_levelForReputation(clamped)];
   619	            if (newLimit > oldLimit) aggregateUplift += newLimit - oldLimit;
   620	            else if (oldLimit > newLimit) aggregateRelease += oldLimit - newLimit;
   621	            // CC-48 round-9 MEDIUM-HIGH-B3: self-healing. A promoted user the migration
   622	            // seed missed carries stock this ledger has never seen, so the FIRST proposal
   623	            // that touches them books their whole standing above-floor limit. Counted as
   624	            // `backfill` rather than as uplift so it cannot spuriously trip the
   625	            // per-proposal cap, which measures what THIS proposal issued.
   626	            if (creditPopulationEpochOf[user] != marker) {
   627	                creditPopulationEpochOf[user] = marker;
   628	                creditPopulationTotal += 1;
   629	                if (oldLimit > floorLimit) backfill += oldLimit - floorLimit;
   630	            }
   631	            globalReputation[user] = clamped;
   632	            lastReputationEpoch[user] = epoch;
   633	            emit GlobalReputationUpdated(user, clamped, epoch);
   634	            unchecked { ++i; }
   635	        }
   636	        uint256 cap = maxAggregateCreditUpliftPerProposal;
   637	        if (aggregateUplift > cap) revert AggregateCreditUpliftExceeded(aggregateUplift, cap);
   638	
   639	        // CC-48 HIGH-1: the per-proposal cap above is only a transaction-level guard --
   640	        // a colluding quorum can emit N proposals in one block and mint N * cap. The real
   641	        // bound is this running stock of outstanding credit exposure, which every proposal
   642	        // is measured against no matter how the work is sliced.
   643	        //
   644	        // The stock is exact rather than approximate because the ONLY other things that can
   645	        // move a user's credit limit -- `setCreditTier` and `setLevelThresholds` -- do not
   646	        // edit this number, they INVALIDATE it (round-9 HIGH-B1): they shut this path until
   647	        // governance re-counts. So between two re-counts the tier schedule is frozen and
   648	        // these deltas are the whole of the movement.
   649	        //
   650	        // Saturating subtraction keeps the running total from ever underflowing. Round-8
   651	        // established the comparison that matters: the alternative to saturating is
   652	        // REVERTING, which would leave the downgrade unapplied and the user holding the
   653	        // HIGHER limit -- strictly worse for the bound. It can only be more conservative.
   654	        uint256 total = totalCreditExposure + aggregateUplift + backfill;
   655	        total = total > aggregateRelease ? total - aggregateRelease : 0;
   656	        uint256 totalCap = maxTotalCreditExposure;
   657	        if (total > totalCap) revert TotalCreditExposureExceeded(total, totalCap);
   658	        totalCreditExposure = total;
   659	
   660	        emit ReputationProposalUplift(proposalId, aggregateUplift, cap, total, totalCap);
   661	    }
   662	
   663	    function updateOperatorBlacklist(
   664	        address operator,
   665	        address[] calldata users,
   666	        bool[] calldata statuses,
   667	        bytes calldata proof
   668	    ) external nonReentrant {
   669	        if (operator == address(0)) revert InvalidParam();
   670	        if (msg.sender != blsAggregator) revert UnauthorizedSource();
   671	        if (users.length != statuses.length) revert LenMismatch();
   672	        if (users.length > 200) revert BatchTooLarge();
   673	        if (SUPER_PAYMASTER == address(0)) revert SPNotSet();
   674	        if (proof.length == 0) revert BLSProofRequired();
   675	
   676	        uint256 nonce = blacklistNonce + 1;
   677	        blacklistNonce = nonce;
   678	
   679	        _verifyBLS(proof, keccak256(abi.encode(
   680	            blsDomainSeparator(), TAG_BLACKLIST, nonce, operator, users, statuses
   681	        )));
   682	
   683	        ISuperPaymaster(SUPER_PAYMASTER).updateBlockedStatus(operator, users, statuses);
   684	    }
   685	
   686	    /// @notice Emitted when the BLS aggregator marks a proposal id executed.
   687	    /// @dev    L-7: gives off-chain monitors an auditable signal for every
   688	    ///         executedProposals write on this path, so a trusted-but-misbehaving
   689	    ///         aggregator pre-locking a proposal id is observable (the call is
   690	    ///         already gated to `blsAggregator`; this adds visibility, not a gate).
   691	    event ProposalMarkedExecuted(uint256 indexed proposalId, address indexed by);
   692	
   693	    function markProposalExecuted(uint256 proposalId) external {
   694	        if (msg.sender != blsAggregator) revert UnauthorizedSource();
   695	        if (proposalId == 0) revert InvalidProposalId();
   696	        executedProposals[proposalId] = true;
   697	        emit ProposalMarkedExecuted(proposalId, msg.sender);
   698	    }
   699	
   700	    /// @notice Set the aPNT credit limit of one reputation level.
   701	    /// @dev    CC-48 round-9 HIGH-B1. This call changes the credit limit of EVERY user
   702	    ///         sitting at `level`, so it necessarily changes protocol-wide exposure -- and
   703	    ///         round-8 let it do that while `totalCreditExposure` sat still, which is how
   704	    ///         one owner call moved the drawable total 16.6x without the protocol-wide cap
   705	    ///         noticing. It is not allowed to leave the ledger behind any more: the
   706	    ///         population is INVALIDATED in the same call (`_invalidateCreditPopulation`),
   707	    ///         which discards the now-meaningless stock and shuts the reputation path until
   708	    ///         governance re-counts. There is no "remember to re-seed afterwards" step to
   709	    ///         forget, because forgetting it stops issuance rather than un-bounding it.
   710	    ///
   711	    ///         Two writes are exempt, and only because neither can move anybody's credit
   712	    ///         limit: re-writing the price a level already has, and pricing a level above
   713	    ///         the currently reachable top (CC-48 round-11 LOW-L1). See the branches
   714	    ///         below for why each is safe; every write that CAN move a limit invalidates.
   715	    ///
    70	
    71	    function version() external pure override returns (string memory) {
    72	        return "Reputation-0.6.0";
    73	    }

    75	    /**
    76	     * @notice Governance sets the Entropy Factor for a community.
    77	     * @dev 1e18 = 1.0. Lower factor increases "resistance" to reputation gain.
    78	     */
    79	    function setEntropyFactor(address community, uint256 factor) external onlyOwner {
    80	        entropyFactors[community] = factor;
    81	        emit EntropyFactorUpdated(community, factor);
    82	    }

    84	    /**
    85	     * @notice Set specific community reputation score (called by DVT/Trusted Source)
    86	     * @dev Allows off-chain calculation results to be stored on-chain for specific communities.
    87	     *      Authorized callers: contract owner OR addresses whitelisted via Registry.isReputationSource().
    88	     */
    89	    function setCommunityReputation(address community, address user, uint256 score) external {
    90	        // Reuse Registry's reputation source whitelist for access control
    91	        if (msg.sender != owner() && !REGISTRY.isReputationSource(msg.sender)) revert Unauthorized();
    92	        communityReputations[community][user] = score;
    93	        emit CommunityReputationUpdated(community, user, score);
    94	    }

    96	    /**
    97	     * @notice Community admins can set their own scoring rules.
    98	     * @dev Restricted to the owner of the community role in the Registry.
    99	     */
   100	    function setRule(bytes32 ruleId, uint256 base, uint256 bonus, uint256 max, string calldata desc) external {
   101	        address community = msg.sender;
   102	        // Verify msg.sender has the COMMUNITY role (multi-tenant: each community manages its own rules)
   103	        if (!REGISTRY.hasRole(keccak256("COMMUNITY"), msg.sender) && owner() != msg.sender) revert NotAuthorized();
   104	        // Bounds constrain rule parameters, not arbitrary activity inputs or entropy factors;
   105	        // multiplication of those inputs may still overflow and revert in computeScore.
   106	        // base/bonus capped at 10_000 points per rule; max capped at 100_000 (max cumulative bonus).
   107	        if (base > 10_000 || bonus > 10_000 || max > 100_000) revert InvalidInput();
   108	
   109	        _ruleListed[community][ruleId] = true;
   110	        bytes32[] storage activeRules = communityActiveRules[community];
   111	        uint256 index = _activeRuleIndex[community][ruleId];
   112	        if (base > 0 && index == 0) {
   113	            activeRules.push(ruleId);
   114	            _activeRuleIndex[community][ruleId] = activeRules.length;
   115	        } else if (base == 0 && index != 0) {
   116	            uint256 last = activeRules.length;
   117	            if (index != last) {
   118	                bytes32 moved = activeRules[last - 1];
   119	                activeRules[index - 1] = moved;
   120	                _activeRuleIndex[community][moved] = index;
   121	            }
   122	            activeRules.pop();
   123	            delete _activeRuleIndex[community][ruleId];
   124	        }
   125	        _ruleActive[community][ruleId] = base > 0;
   126	        communityRules[community][ruleId] = Rule(base, bonus, max, desc);
   127	        emit RuleUpdated(community, ruleId, base, bonus);
   128	    }
   129	
   130	    function setNFTBoost(address collection, uint256 boost) external onlyOwner {
   131	        if (collection == address(0)) revert InvalidAddress();
   132	        if (!_collectionListed[collection]) {
   133	            if (boostedCollections.length >= MAX_BOOSTED_COLLECTIONS) revert MaxBoostedReached();
   134	            _collectionListed[collection] = true;
   135	            boostedCollections.push(collection);
   136	        }
   137	        nftCollectionBoost[collection] = boost;
   138	        emit NFTBoostAdded(collection, boost);
   139	    }
   140	
   141	    /**
   142	     * @notice Compute reputation for a user based on their community activities.
   143	     * @dev Activities are now mapped to rules.
   144	     */
   145	    function computeScore(
   146	        address user,
   147	        address[] memory communities,
   148	        bytes32[][] memory ruleIds,
   149	        uint256[][] memory activities
   150	    ) public view returns (uint256 totalScore) {
   151	        if (ruleIds.length != communities.length || activities.length != communities.length) revert InvalidInput();
   152	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < communities.length; i++) {
   153	            if (ruleIds[i].length != activities[i].length) revert InvalidInput();
   154	            address community = communities[i];
   155	            uint256 communityScore = 0;
   156	
   157	            for (uint256 j = 0; j < ruleIds[i].length; j++) {
   158	                bytes32 rId = ruleIds[i][j];
   159	                Rule memory r = communityRules[community][rId];
   160	
   161	                // A configured zero-base rule is disabled, not missing.
   162	                if (_ruleListed[community][rId] && !_ruleActive[community][rId]) continue;
   163	
   164	                // If rule was never configured, use the default for the first rule.
   165	                // P1-26 resolved: fallback uses constructor-initialized defaultRule; no setter changes it
   166	                if (!_ruleListed[community][rId] && j == 0) {
   167	                    r = defaultRule;
   168	                }
   169	
   170	                uint256 bonus = activities[i][j] * r.activityBonus;
   171	                if (bonus > r.maxBonus) bonus = r.maxBonus;
   172	                communityScore += r.baseScore + bonus;
   173	            }
   174	
   175	            // Apply Entropy Factor (1e18 = 1.0)
   176	            uint256 factor = entropyFactors[community];
   177	            if (factor == 0) factor = 1e18; // Default to 1.0
   178	
   179	            totalScore += (communityScore * factor) / 1e18;
   180	        }
   181	
   182	        // Global NFT Boosts
   183	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < boostedCollections.length; i++) {
   184	            address collection = boostedCollections[i];
   185	            if (_nftBoostEligible(user, collection)) totalScore += nftCollectionBoost[collection];
   186	        }
   187	    }
   188	
   189	    /**
   190	     * @notice Sync computed reputation score to the Registry for the given user.
   250	        if (netAmount > 0) {
   251	            GTOKEN.safeTransfer(user, netAmount);
   252	        }
   253	        
   254	        emit StakeUnlocked(user, roleId, originalAmount, exitFee, netAmount, block.timestamp);
   255	
   256	        // P0-14: lock has been deleted; reflect 0 in Registry's cache.
   257	        // Registry.exitRole already sets `roleStakes[roleId][user] = 0`
   258	        // before calling here, but the redundant sync makes the
   259	        // post-condition explicit and safe under future call-graph changes.
   260	        _syncRegistry(user, roleId, 0);
   261	
   262	        return netAmount;
   263	    }

   265	    /**
   266	     * @notice Slash a user
   267	     */
   268	    function slash(
   269	        address user,
   270	        uint256 amount,
   271	        string calldata reason
   272	    ) external nonReentrant returns (uint256 slashedAmount) {
   273	        if (msg.sender != REGISTRY && !authorizedSlashers[msg.sender]) {
   274	            revert OnlyRegistryOrAuthorized();
   275	        }
   276	
   277	        StakeInfo storage info = stakes[user];
   278	        
   279	        // H-01 FIX: Calculate available balance correctly
   280	        // Since info.amount is now NET of slashes, the available amount IS the info.amount
   281	        uint256 available = info.amount;
   282	        slashedAmount = amount > available ? available : amount;
   283	
   284	        if (slashedAmount > 0) {
   285	            // H-01 FIX: Synchronize both fields to prevent underflow
   286	            info.slashedAmount += slashedAmount;  // Track cumulative slashed
   287	            info.amount -= slashedAmount;         // Reduce actual balance
   288	            totalStaked -= slashedAmount;
   289	
   290	            // Proportionally reduce role locks (Weighted Distribution)
   291	            bytes32[] storage roles = userActiveRoles[user];
   292	            uint256 totalAmountAcrossLocks = info.amount + slashedAmount; // Amount BEFORE this slash
   293	
   294	            // P0-14: snapshot the affected role list BEFORE _cleanupZeroLocks
   295	            // mutates it, so we can sync each role's post-slash amount to
   296	            // Registry. Roles are bytes32 — a memory copy is cheap.
   297	            bytes32[] memory affectedRoles = new bytes32[](roles.length);
   298	            uint256[] memory postSlashAmounts = new uint256[](roles.length);
   299	
   300	            if (totalAmountAcrossLocks > 0) {
   440	        uint256 amount = roleLocks[user][roleId].amount;
   441	        return _previewExitFee(user, roleId, amount);
   442	    }

   454	    function setRoleExitFee(bytes32 roleId, uint256 feePercent, uint256 minFee) external {
   455	        if (msg.sender != REGISTRY && msg.sender != owner()) revert Unauthorized();
   456	        // P1-41: mirror Registry.configureRole 20% cap to prevent owner setting ruinous exit fees
   457	        if (feePercent > 2000) revert FeeTooHigh();
   458	        RoleExitConfig storage config = roleExitConfigs[roleId];
   459	        config.feePercent = feePercent;
   460	        config.minFee = minFee;
   461	        emit RoleExitFeeUpdated(roleId, feePercent, minFee);
   462	    }

   491	    /**
   492	     * @notice Slash operator's stake (DVT Validator only)
   493	     * @param operator Operator to slash
   494	     * @param roleId Role being slashed
   495	     * @param penaltyAmount Amount of GToken to slash
   496	     * @param reason Reason for slashing
   497	     */
   498	    function slashByDVT(
   499	        address operator,
   500	        bytes32 roleId,
   501	        uint256 penaltyAmount,
   502	        string calldata reason
   503	    ) external nonReentrant {
   504	        if (!authorizedSlashers[msg.sender]) revert NotAuthorizedSlasher();
   505	
   506	        RoleLock storage lock = roleLocks[operator][roleId];
   507	        if (lock.amount < penaltyAmount) revert InsufficientStake();
   508	
   509	        // Deduct from role lock
   510	        lock.amount -= uint128(penaltyAmount);
   511	
   512	        // H-01 FIX: Deduct from stake and track cumulative slashed
   513	        StakeInfo storage stake = stakes[operator];
   514	        if (stake.amount < penaltyAmount) revert InsufficientStake();
   515	        stake.slashedAmount += penaltyAmount;  // Track cumulative slashed
   450	        emergencyQueuedAt = 0;
   451	        emergencyPendingPrice = 0;
   452	
   453	        emit EmergencyPriceExecuted(newPrice);
   454	        emit PriceUpdated(newPrice, block.timestamp, msg.sender);
   455	    }
   456	
   457	    /**
   458	     * @notice Update price via DVT/BLS consensus or the owner (Safe) break-glass path.
   459	     * @dev The BLS aggregator is capped per update to ±20% of the cached price. The owner is
   460	     *      uncapped within [MIN_ETH_USD_PRICE, MAX_ETH_USD_PRICE]. Both callers remain subject to
   461	     *      two-hour recency, future-timestamp grace, strictly increasing updatedAt, and the
   462	     *      Chainlink ±20% check when Chainlink is fresh.
   463	     * @param chainlinkRecovered 0 = Chainlink still unavailable; 1 = recovered (clears priceMode).
   464	     */
   465	    function updatePriceDVT(int256 price, uint256 updatedAt, bytes calldata proof, uint8 chainlinkRecovered) external {
   466	        proof; // verified by BLSAggregator before it calls this function
   467	        // 1. Verify caller authority (owner path = emergency break-glass; Chainlink ±20% guard below)
   468	        if (msg.sender != BLS_AGGREGATOR && msg.sender != owner()) revert Unauthorized();
   469	
   470	        // V3.6 FIX: Prevent Replay & Staleness
   471	        if (updatedAt <= cachedPrice.updatedAt) revert OracleError(); // Must be strictly increasing
   472	        if (updatedAt < block.timestamp - 2 hours) revert OracleError(); // Must be recent
   473	        // P0-16: reject future timestamps (15 s keeper clock-skew grace).
   474	        if (updatedAt > block.timestamp + TIMESTAMP_GRACE_SECONDS) revert OracleError();
   475	
   476	        // 3. Validate absolute bounds and the per-update BLS movement cap.
   477	        if (price < MIN_ETH_USD_PRICE || price > MAX_ETH_USD_PRICE) revert OracleError();
   478	        if (msg.sender == BLS_AGGREGATOR && msg.sender != owner()) {
   479	            int256 referencePrice = cachedPrice.price;
   480	            if (referencePrice > 0) {
   481	                int256 delta = price > referencePrice ? price - referencePrice : referencePrice - price;
   482	                if (delta * int256(BPS_DENOMINATOR) > referencePrice * int256(EMERGENCY_PRICE_DEVIATION_BPS)) {
   483	                    revert OracleError();
   484	                }
   485	            }
   486	        }
   487	
   488	        // 4. Deviation from Chainlink (±20%) when Chainlink is recent
   489	        try ETH_USD_PRICE_FEED.latestRoundData() returns (
   490	            uint80, int256 chainlinkPrice, uint256, uint256 chainlinkUpdatedAt, uint80
   491	        ) {
   492	            // P1-42: guard chainlinkPrice <= 0 to prevent div-by-zero
   493	            if (chainlinkPrice > 0 && block.timestamp - chainlinkUpdatedAt < 2 hours) {
   494	                int256 delta = price > chainlinkPrice ? price - chainlinkPrice : chainlinkPrice - price;
   495	                if (delta * int256(BPS_DENOMINATOR) > chainlinkPrice * int256(EMERGENCY_PRICE_DEVIATION_BPS)) {
   496	                    revert OracleError();
   497	                }
   498	            }
   499	        } catch {
   500	            // Chainlink down: DVT price accepted without deviation check
   501	        }
   502	
   503	        // 5. Update cache
   504	        cachedPrice = PriceCache({
   505	            price: price,
   506	            updatedAt: updatedAt,
   507	            roundId: 0, // DVT doesn't have Chainlink RoundID
   508	            decimals: 8 // DVT normalizes to 8 decimals
   509	        });
   510	
   511	        if (chainlinkRecovered == 1 && priceMode != 0) {
   512	            emit PriceModeChanged(priceMode, 0, msg.sender);
   513	            priceMode = 0;
   514	            emergencyActivatedAt = 0;
   515	        }
   516	
   517	        emit PriceUpdated(price, updatedAt, msg.sender);
   518	    }
   519	
   520	    /// @notice Update price cache from Chainlink oracle (keeper-callable).
   521	    function updatePrice() external {
   522	        try ETH_USD_PRICE_FEED.latestRoundData() returns (
   523	            uint80 roundId, int256 price, uint256, uint256 updatedAt, uint80 answeredInRound
   524	        ) {
   525	            if (price < MIN_ETH_USD_PRICE || price > MAX_ETH_USD_PRICE) revert OracleError();
   526	            if (updatedAt < block.timestamp - priceStalenessThreshold) revert OracleError();
   527	            if (answeredInRound < roundId) revert OracleError();
   528	
   529	            cachedPrice = PriceCache({price: price, updatedAt: updatedAt, roundId: roundId, decimals: 8});
   530	
   531	            // P0-10: Chainlink came back — leave EMERGENCY mode and drop any queued override.
   532	            if (priceMode != 0) {
   533	                emit PriceModeChanged(priceMode, 0, msg.sender);
   534	                priceMode = 0;
   535	                emergencyActivatedAt = 0;
   536	            }
   537	            if (emergencyQueuedAt != 0) {
   538	                int256 cancelled = emergencyPendingPrice;
   539	                emergencyQueuedAt = 0;
   540	                emergencyPendingPrice = 0;
   541	                emit EmergencyPriceCancelled(cancelled);
   542	            }
   543	
   544	            emit PriceUpdated(price, updatedAt, msg.sender);
   545	        } catch {
   546	            // Chainlink down: keeper should call updatePriceDVT() with BLS proof, or the owner can
   547	            // use emergencySetPrice + executeEmergencyPrice.
   548	            revert OracleError();
   549	        }
   550	    }
