   552	    // ====================================
   553	    // Credit view
   554	    // ====================================
   555	
   556	    /// @notice Remaining credit headroom for `user` on `token` (saturating; spec §10.4 / R4-H4).
   557	    function getAvailableCredit(address user, address token) external view returns (uint256) {
   558	        IxPNTsTokenV2 t = IxPNTsTokenV2(token);
   559	        uint256 cap = t.effectiveCreditCap(user);
   560	        uint256 used = t.debts(user) + t.creditReservedOf(user);
   561	        return cap > used ? cap - used : 0;
   562	    }
   563	
   564	    // ====================================
   565	    // Reputation & Slash Management
   566	    // ====================================
   567	
   568	    /**
   569	     * @notice Mark an operator as having a pending slash (owner, BLS aggregator, or guardian).
   570	     * @dev M-5: an owner or BLS queue blocks requestWithdraw/withdraw until the slash executes or is
   571	     *      cancelled. An unconfirmed guardian queue blocks only until queuedAt + GUARDIAN_SLASH_FREEZE
   572	     *      (7 days) and then expires (isSlashPending() turns false); owner/BLS confirmation makes it
   573	     *      persistent. Guardian repeats revert while unresolved. Owner repeats overwrite provenance and
   574	     *      time; BLS repeats do the same outside its cooldown.
   575	     */
   576	    function queueSlash(address operator) external {
   577	        address actor = msg.sender;
   578	        bool byGuardian = actor == guardian && actor != address(0) && actor != owner() && actor != BLS_AGGREGATOR;
   579	        if (actor != owner() && actor != BLS_AGGREGATOR && !byGuardian) revert Unauthorized();
   580	        if (byGuardian) {
   581	            if (slashQueues[operator].guardianQueued) revert GuardianSlashUnresolved();
   582	            if (_isSlashPending(operator)) revert SlashPending();
   583	        } else if (actor == BLS_AGGREGATOR && actor != owner() && uint48(block.timestamp) < _blsCooldownEnd(operator)) {
   584	            revert SlashCooldown();
   585	        }
   586	        _pendingSlash[operator] = true;
   587	        slashQueues[operator] = SlashQueue(actor, uint48(block.timestamp), byGuardian);
   588	        emit SlashQueued(operator, msg.sender);
   589	    }
   590	
   591	    /// @dev Effective end of the BLS-slash cooldown: max(per-operator cooldown, global floor).
   592	    function _blsCooldownEnd(address operator) internal view returns (uint48) {
   593	        uint48 cd = _blsSlashCd[operator];
   594	        uint48 fl = _blsSlashCdFloor;
   595	        return fl > cd ? fl : cd;
   596	    }
   597	
   598	    /// @notice One-shot prime of the global BLS-slash cooldown floor to `now + SLASH_BLS_COOLDOWN`.
   599	    function primeBlsSlashCooldown() external onlyOwner {
   600	        _blsSlashCdFloor = uint48(block.timestamp) + SLASH_BLS_COOLDOWN;
   601	        emit BlsSlashCooldownPrimed(_blsSlashCdFloor);
   602	    }
   603	
   604	    /// @notice Cancel a previously queued slash (owner only). Idempotent; emits only when a flag or
   605	    ///         a queue record (including an expired guardian queue) was actually cleared.
   606	    function cancelSlash(address operator) external onlyOwner {
   607	        if (!_pendingSlash[operator] && slashQueues[operator].queuedAt == 0) return;
   608	        _clearSlashQueue(operator);
   609	        emit SlashCancelled(operator, msg.sender);
   610	    }
   611	
   612	    /// @notice Whether `operator` currently has an effective withdrawal-blocking slash status.
   613	    function isSlashPending(address operator) external view returns (bool) {
   614	        return _isSlashPending(operator);
   615	    }
   616	
   617	    /**
   618	     * @notice Slash an operator (Admin/Governance only). Requires queueSlash first; 24h cooldown.
   619	     */
   620	    function slashOperator(
   621	        address operator,
   622	        ISuperPaymaster.SlashLevel level,
   623	        uint256 penaltyAmount,
   624	        string calldata reason
   625	    ) external onlyOwner {
   626	        _slashOperator(operator, level, penaltyAmount, reason);
   627	    }
   628	
   629	    /// @notice Atomically release supplied stale reservations and slash their operator.
   630	    function slashOperatorAfterReleasing(
   631	        address operator,
   632	        ISuperPaymaster.SlashLevel level,
   633	        uint256 penaltyAmount,
   634	        string calldata reason,
   635	        bytes32[] calldata staleOpHashes
   636	    ) external onlyOwner {
   637	        _releaseSuppliedStale(operator, staleOpHashes);
   638	        _slashOperator(operator, level, penaltyAmount, reason);
   639	    }
   640	
   641	    function _slashOperator(
   642	        address operator,
   643	        ISuperPaymaster.SlashLevel level,
   644	        uint256 penaltyAmount,
   645	        string memory reason
   646	    ) internal {
   647	        require(_isSlashPending(operator), "SP: must queueSlash first");
   648	        if (uint48(block.timestamp) < _slashCd[operator]) revert SlashCooldown();
   649	        _slashCd[operator] = uint48(block.timestamp) + 24 hours;
   650	        _slash(operator, level, penaltyAmount, reason, true);
   651	        _clearSlashQueue(operator);
   652	    }
   653	
   654	    /// @notice Update Operator Reputation (External Credit Manager)
   655	    function updateReputation(address operator, uint256 newScore) external onlyOwner {
   656	        if (newScore > type(uint32).max) revert ScoreExceedsUint32();
   657	        operators[operator].reputation = uint32(newScore);
   658	        emit ISuperPaymaster.ReputationUpdated(operator, newScore, msg.sender);
   659	    }
   660	
   661	    /**
   662	     * @notice Execute slash triggered by BLS consensus (DVT Module only)
   663	     */
   664	    function executeSlashWithBLS(address operator, ISuperPaymaster.SlashLevel level, bytes calldata proof) external {
   665	        if (msg.sender != BLS_AGGREGATOR) revert Unauthorized();
   666	        _executeSlashWithBLS(operator, level, proof);
   667	    }
   668	
   669	    function _executeSlashWithBLS(address operator, ISuperPaymaster.SlashLevel level, bytes memory proof) internal {
   670	        require(_isSlashPending(operator), "SP: must queueSlash first");
   671	        if (uint48(block.timestamp) < _blsCooldownEnd(operator)) revert SlashCooldown();
   672	        uint256 reserved = inflightReserved[operator];
   673	        if (reserved != 0) revert SlashBlockedByInflight(reserved);
   674	        _blsSlashCd[operator] = uint48(block.timestamp) + SLASH_BLS_COOLDOWN;
   675	
   676	        uint256 collateral = uint256(operators[operator].aPNTsBalance) + pendingWithdrawals[operator].amount;
   677	        // Logical penalty before cap: Warning=0, Minor=10%, Major=full collateral (capped at 30% in _slash).
   678	        uint256 penalty = 0;
   679	        if (level == ISuperPaymaster.SlashLevel.MINOR) {
   680	            penalty = collateral / 10;

   189	    /**
   190	     * @notice Sync computed reputation score to the Registry for the given user.
   191	     * @dev Computes score via computeScore then calls Registry.batchUpdateGlobalReputation.
   192	     *      The proposalId is derived deterministically from (user, epoch) to ensure uniqueness.
   193	     */
   194	    function syncToRegistry(
   195	        address user,
   196	        address[] memory communities,
   197	        bytes32[][] memory ruleIds,
   198	        uint256[][] memory activities,
   199	        uint256 epoch,
   200	        bytes calldata proof
   201	    ) external {
   202	        uint256 score = computeScore(user, communities, ruleIds, activities);
   203	        address[] memory users = new address[](1);
   204	        users[0] = user;
   205	        uint256[] memory scores = new uint256[](1);
   206	        scores[0] = score;
   207	
   208	        // Hash-derived ID for (user, epoch). Separation from arbitrary external IDs
   209	        // and nonzero output are probabilistic, not structurally guaranteed.
   210	        // Registry independently verifies the full-width ID and enforces user epochs.
   211	        uint256 proposalId = uint256(keccak256(abi.encode("RS", user, epoch)));
   212	        REGISTRY.batchUpdateGlobalReputation(proposalId, users, scores, epoch, proof);
   213	        emit ReputationComputed(user, score);
   214	    }

   219	    function calculateReputation(

   221	        address community,
   222	        uint256 /* sbtTokenId */

   232	        ruleIds[0] = rules;

   235	        activities[0] = new uint256[](rules.length); // No activity data: every allocated entry is zero
   236	
   237	        address[] memory communities = new address[](1);
   238	        communities[0] = community;
   239	
   240	        communityScore = computeScore(user, communities, ruleIds, activities);
   241	        globalScore = communityScore; // Simplification for now
   242	    }

   244	    function getReputationBreakdown(
   245	        address user,
   246	        address community,
   247	        uint256 /* sbtTokenId */
   248	    )
   249	        external
   250	        view
   251	        override
   252	        returns (uint256 baseScore, uint256 nftBonus, uint256 activityBonus, uint256 multiplier)
   253	    {
   254	        // Mirror calculateReputation: all active rules, zero activity inputs,
   255	        // then the community multiplier and global NFT boosts.
   256	        bytes32[] memory rules = communityActiveRules[community];
   257	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < rules.length; i++) {
   258	            baseScore += communityRules[community][rules[i]].baseScore;
   259	        }
   260	
   261	        multiplier = entropyFactors[community];
   262	        if (multiplier == 0) multiplier = 1e18;
   263	        baseScore = (baseScore * multiplier) / 1e18;
   264	        activityBonus = 0;
   265	
   266	        // Apply the same registration-age and current-ownership predicate as computeScore.
   267	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < boostedCollections.length; i++) {
   268	            address collection = boostedCollections[i];
   269	            if (_nftBoostEligible(user, collection)) nftBonus += nftCollectionBoost[collection];
   270	        }
   650	        // Saturating subtraction keeps the running total from ever underflowing. Round-8
   651	        // established the comparison that matters: the alternative to saturating is
   652	        // REVERTING, which would leave the downgrade unapplied and the user holding the
   653	        // HIGHER limit -- strictly worse for the bound. It can only be more conservative.
   654	        uint256 total = totalCreditExposure + aggregateUplift + backfill;
   655	        total = total > aggregateRelease ? total - aggregateRelease : 0;
   656	        uint256 totalCap = maxTotalCreditExposure;
   657	        if (total > totalCap) revert TotalCreditExposureExceeded(total, totalCap);
   658	        totalCreditExposure = total;
   659	
   660	        emit ReputationProposalUplift(proposalId, aggregateUplift, cap, total, totalCap);
   661	    }

   430	    /// @dev Reputation sources are exactly ReputationSystem and the aggregator. The aggregator's
   431	    ///      verifyAndExecute reputation branch (floor-checked) needs the role; the generic path can no
   432	    ///      longer reach batchUpdateGlobalReputation (H-1 denylist). Registry.initialize makes its
   433	    ///      owner (the deployer) a source (Registry.sol:172), so that grant is revoked here.
   434	    function _wireReputationSources() internal {
   435	        registry.setReputationSource(address(repSystem), true);
   436	        registry.setReputationSource(address(aggregator), true);
   437	        registry.setReputationSource(deployer, false);
   438	    }
   439	
   440	    /// @dev Same expectation as the Sepolia manifest (step 13): ReputationSystem and the aggregator are
   441	    ///      sources; the deployer and every other address this script deploys or wires are not.
   442	    function _assertReputationSources() internal view {
   443	        require(registry.isReputationSource(address(repSystem)), "wire: registry.setReputationSource(repSystem)");
   444	        require(registry.isReputationSource(address(aggregator)), "wire: registry.setReputationSource(aggregator)");
   445	        address[22] memory notSources = [
   446	            deployer, address(superPaymaster), spImplAddr, address(dvt), address(staking), address(mysbt),
   447	            address(gtoken), address(xpntsFactory), address(registry), registryImplAddr, address(apnts),
   448	            address(pmFactory), address(pmV4Impl), address(microPaymentCh), x402FacilitatorAddr,
   449	            policyRegistryAddr, v55.factory, v55.impl, v55.ext, v55.lens, v55.tierSource, v55.aoaRegistry
   450	        ];
   451	        for (uint256 i; i < notSources.length; ++i) {
   452	            if (notSources[i] == address(0)) continue;
   453	            require(!registry.isReputationSource(notSources[i]), "wire: unexpected reputation source");
   454	        }
   455	    }
   456	
   457	    function _executeWiring() internal {
   458	        registry.setSuperPaymaster(address(superPaymaster));
   459	        _wireReputationSources();
   460	        registry.setBLSAggregator(address(aggregator));
   461	        dvt.setBLSAggregator(address(aggregator));
   462	        aggregator.setDVTValidator(address(dvt));
   463	        pmFactory.addImplementation("v4.2", address(pmV4Impl));
   464	        // 5.5.0 (runbook step 6): SP binds operators to tokens issued by xPNTsFactoryV2.
   465	        _wireSPFactory(address(superPaymaster), v55.factory);
   466	        xpntsFactory.setSuperPaymasterAddress(address(superPaymaster));
   467	        // Wire aPNTs (deployed before SP in Step 2, so SP address must be set retroactively).
   468	        // setSuperPaymasterAddress also sets autoApprovedSpenders[SP] = true.
   469	        apnts.setSuperPaymasterAddress(address(superPaymaster));
   470	
   471	        // Wire BLSAggregator into SuperPaymaster (Tier 1 slash authorization).
   472	        // Uses initBLSAggregator() — a one-time setter that bypasses the 24h timelock
   473	        // because BLS_AGGREGATOR is address(0) on a fresh deployment (no governance asset to protect).
   474	        superPaymaster.initBLSAggregator(address(aggregator));
   475	
   180	        SuperPaymaster spImpl = SuperPaymaster(
   181	            payable(_deployDefault("SuperPaymaster", abi.encode(entryPointAddr, address(registry), priceFeedAddr)))
   182	        );
   183	        spImplAddr = address(spImpl);
   184	        bytes memory spInit = abi.encodeCall(SuperPaymaster.initialize, (deployer, address(apnts), deployer, 4200));
   185	        superPaymaster = SuperPaymaster(payable(_deployDefault("ERC1967Proxy", abi.encode(address(spImpl), spInit))));
   186	
   187	        console.log("=== Step 5b: Deploy xPNTs v2 stack (runbook step 4) ===");
   188	        v55 = _ensureV55Stack(v55, address(superPaymaster), address(registry), deployer);
   189	
   190	        console.log("=== Step 6: Deploy Other Modules ===");
   191	        repSystem = ReputationSystem(_deployDefault("ReputationSystem", abi.encode(address(registry))));
   192	        // DVTValidator must be deployed before BLSAggregator (constructor rejects address(0))
   193	        dvt = DVTValidator(
   194	            _deployDefault(
   195	                "DVTValidator",
   196	                abi.encode(address(registry), uint256(1), new bytes32[](0), new address[](0))
   197	            )
   198	        );
   199	        aggregator = BLSAggregator(
   200	            _deployDefault("BLSAggregator", abi.encode(address(registry), address(superPaymaster), address(dvt)))
   201	        );
   202	        pmFactory = PaymasterFactory(_deployDefault("PaymasterFactory", ""));
   203	        pmV4Impl = Paymaster(payable(_deployDefault("Paymaster", abi.encode(address(registry)))));
   204	        microPaymentCh = MicroPaymentChannel(_deployDefault("MicroPaymentChannel", abi.encode(deployer)));
   205	        accountFactory = SimpleAccountFactory(_deployDefault("SimpleAccountFactory", abi.encode(entryPointAddr)));
   320	        _verifyV55Stack(v55, address(superPaymaster), address(registry));
   321	        _verifySPFactory(address(superPaymaster), v55.factory);
   322	        _verifyV2Token(aastarXPNTsV2, v55.factory, deployer, address(superPaymaster));
   323	        _verifyV2Token(demoXPNTsV2, v55.factory, anni, address(superPaymaster));
   324	        _verifyOperatorV2(address(superPaymaster), deployer, aastarXPNTsV2, deployer);
   325	        _verifyOperatorV2(address(superPaymaster), anni, demoXPNTsV2, anni);
   326	        _verifyPriceFresh(address(superPaymaster));
   327	        require(_strEq(superPaymaster.version(), SP_V55_VERSION), "DeployAnvil: SP is not 5.5.0");
   328	        // AUD-4: the proxy's live implementation must be the profile.default SuperPaymaster.
   329	        require(_implOf(address(superPaymaster)) == spImplAddr, "DeployAnvil: SP proxy impl != deployed impl");
   330	        _requireDefaultArtifact(spImplAddr, "SuperPaymaster");
   331	
   332	        GovernanceOwnerGate.requireGovernanceOwner(address(aggregator), aggregator.owner(), "BLSAggregator");
   333	        require(dvt.owner() == aggregator.owner(), "DeployAnvil: DVT owner != BLS owner");
   334	        require(dvt.categoryCount() == 0, "DeployAnvil: dvt evidence allowlist must be empty in 5.5.0");
   335	        // Not wrapped in a `declaredGovernanceOwner() != 0` check, and the aggregator's
   336	        // is not either. With the variable unset the gate REFUSES rather than passing,
   337	        // even when the owner is already a Safe -- it proves "this is the Safe the
   338	        // operator declared", not "this is a Safe" (pinned by
   339	        // test_SafeOwnedSubjectIsStillRefusedWhenNothingWasDeclared). So a guard here
   340	        // was redundant, and leaving it in meant the three subjects were gated by two
   341	        // different rules for no reason. Raised by pr-daemon on #404.
   342	        GovernanceOwnerGate.requireGovernanceOwner(address(apnts), apnts.communityOwner(), "aPNTs");
   343	        GovernanceOwnerGate.requireGovernanceOwner(address(xpntsFactory), xpntsFactory.owner(), "xPNTsFactory");
   344	        _assertAllDefaultArtifacts();
   345	        _generateConfig();
   346	    }

   348	    /// @notice T-4 closing check: every contract this script created — directly, through a
   349	    ///         proxy, or as a factory clone — runs the profile.default bytes. View-only.

   364	        _requireDefaultArtifact(address(repSystem), "ReputationSystem");

   384	        );

   387	    function _executeWiring() internal {
   388	        registry.setSuperPaymaster(address(superPaymaster));
   389	        registry.setReputationSource(address(repSystem), true);
   390	        // The DVT reputation path calls Registry from BLSAggregator after the
   391	        // first threshold verification. Registry independently verifies the
   392	        // same proof, so the aggregator itself must be an authorized source.
   393	        registry.setReputationSource(address(aggregator), true);
   394	        registry.setBLSAggregator(address(aggregator));
   395	        dvt.setBLSAggregator(address(aggregator));
   396	        aggregator.setDVTValidator(address(dvt));
   397	
   398	        // CRITICAL: Update factory's SuperPaymaster address
   399	        xpntsFactory.setSuperPaymasterAddress(address(superPaymaster));
   400	
   401	        // Wire BLSAggregator into SuperPaymaster (Tier 1 slash authorization — one-time, no timelock).
   402	        superPaymaster.initBLSAggregator(address(aggregator));
   403	
   404	        // 5.5.0 is disclosure-only: mirror DeployLive, the aggregator holds no staking slasher role.
   405	        require(!staking.authorizedSlashers(address(aggregator)), "DeployAnvil: aggregator must not be a staking slasher");
   406	
   407	        // Configure auto-approval for aPNTs (already deployed via factory)
   408	        apnts.setSuperPaymasterAddress(address(superPaymaster));
   409	
   410	        pmFactory.addImplementation("v4.2", address(pmV4Impl));

   223	    function _deployOne(bytes32 category, address checkerAddress) internal returns (DVTValidator deployed) {
   224	        bytes32[] memory categories = new bytes32[](1);
   225	        categories[0] = category;
   226	        address[] memory checkers = new address[](1);
   227	        checkers[0] = checkerAddress;
   228	        deployed = new DVTValidator(address(registry), 1, categories, checkers);
   229	    }

   231	    function _evidence(bytes32 category) internal pure returns (bytes memory) {
   232	        return abi.encode(category, BODY);
   233	    }

   236	        return keccak256(abi.encode(keccak256("SuperPaymaster.DVT.Evidence.v2"), category, keccak256(BODY)));
   237	    }

  1628	    ///         still buys is the direction that matters: ARMING or REPLACING the
  1629	    ///         authority behind a queue-time-fraction slash is publicly visible for five days
  1630	    ///         before it can act. It is deliberately NOT applied to disarming — see
  1631	    ///         `emergencyDisarmFraudProofVerifier`, which is immediate because it only
  1632	    ///         ever removes power (CC-48 round-5 MEDIUM-2).
  1633	    /// @dev    ROLE_DVT exit is now gated by requestGuardianExit + a bounded
  1634	    ///         unbonding delay. The guardian leaves valid signer masks at readyAt and can
  1635	    ///         withdraw only after a further GUARDIAN_FRAUD_CHALLENGE; a verifier-approved
  1636	    ///         queued case increments the pending counter and Registry cannot consume the exit until all cases
  1637	    ///         resolve. Governance must wire this aggregator into Registry before
  1638	    ///         arming the verifier.
  1639	    function proposeFraudProofVerifier(address verifier) external onlyOwner {
  1640	        uint64 readyAt = uint64(block.timestamp + VERIFIER_ROTATION_DELAY);
  1641	        pendingFraudProofVerifier = verifier;
  1642	        pendingFraudProofVerifierReadyAt = readyAt;
  1643	        emit FraudProofVerifierRotationProposed(verifier, readyAt);
  1644	    }
  1645	
  1646	    /// @notice Abandon an in-flight verifier rotation.
  1647	    function cancelFraudProofVerifierRotation() external onlyOwner {
  1648	        if (pendingFraudProofVerifierReadyAt == 0) revert NoPendingVerifierRotation();
  1649	        emit FraudProofVerifierRotationCancelled(pendingFraudProofVerifier);
  1650	        delete pendingFraudProofVerifier;
  1651	        delete pendingFraudProofVerifierReadyAt;
  1652	    }
  1653	
  1654	    /// @notice Finalise a matured verifier rotation.
  1655	    /// @dev    Permissionless on purpose: the decision was already taken by `owner`
  1656	    ///         and has served its full delay, so anyone may push the button. Keeping
  1657	    ///         it owner-only would just hand the owner a second, unbounded veto.
  1658	    function applyFraudProofVerifier() external {
  1659	        uint64 readyAt = pendingFraudProofVerifierReadyAt;
  1660	        if (readyAt == 0) revert NoPendingVerifierRotation();
  1661	        if (block.timestamp < uint256(readyAt)) revert VerifierRotationNotReady(uint256(readyAt));
  1662	        address next = pendingFraudProofVerifier;
  1663	        delete pendingFraudProofVerifier;
  1664	        delete pendingFraudProofVerifierReadyAt;
  1665	        emit FraudProofVerifierUpdated(fraudProofVerifier, next);
  1666	        fraudProofVerifier = next;
  1667	    }
  1668	
  1669	    /// @notice EMERGENCY: disarm the fraud-proof verifier immediately — no delay.
  1670	    ///         Clears the active verifier AND any in-flight rotation, so no new
  1671	    ///         guardian-slash case can be opened from this block onwards.
  1672	    /// @dev    CC-48 round-5 MEDIUM-2. Before this existed, the only way to take a
  1673	    ///         misbehaving verifier off-line was `proposeFraudProofVerifier(0)` +
  1674	    ///         `VERIFIER_ROTATION_DELAY` (5 days) + `applyFraudProofVerifier`. A
  1675	    ///         compromised verifier can open a case and have its configured penalty
  1676	    ///         executed against every accused guardian within a SINGLE block, so a five-day
  1677	    ///         remedy was not a remedy. This call is the answer to "the verifier is
  1678	    ///         lying, stop it now".
  1679	    ///
  1680	    ///         WHAT IT DOES NOT TOUCH, by construction rather than by promise: since
  1681	    ///         round-4, `executeGuardianSlash` / `retry` / `expire` read ONLY the verdict
  1682	    ///         frozen in `guardianSlashCases[id]` and never read `fraudProofVerifier`
  1683	    ///         (line ~1730 below is its single non-governance read, inside
  1684	    ///         `queueGuardianSlash`). Disarming cannot cancel, stall, or re-judge an
  1685	    ///         already-queued case. Cases opened BEFORE the disarm run to their frozen
  1686	    ///         verdict; use `expireGuardianSlashCase` after the deadline to drop one.
  1687	    ///
  1688	    ///         RESIDUAL RISK — THIS IS A NEW OWNER POWER (CC-48 round-6 HIGH-1). Earlier
  1689	    ///         revisions of this comment claimed a stolen `owner` "gains nothing new"
  1690	    ///         because `proposeFraudProofVerifier(0)` + 5 days reached the same terminal
  1691	    ///         state. That is FALSE in the only dimension that matters here — TIME — and
  1692	    ///         it is retracted:
  1693	    ///           • Before 4.9.0 NO owner entry point could block `queueGuardianSlash` in
  1694	    ///             the same block. Its preconditions (`verifier != 0`,
  1695	    ///             `verifier.code.length != 0`, `fraudProofId != 0`, `status == 0`,
  1696	    ///             `_validateGuardianSet`, `verify()`) are untouched by every other
  1697	    ///             onlyOwner function; `_validateGuardianSet` is `pure`, so even
  1698	    ///             `revokeBLSPublicKey` / `releaseKeyBinding` cannot stop a case opening.
  1699	    ///           • From 4.9.0 the owner CAN, immediately and with no public notice,
  1700	    ///             censor ALL FUTURE accusations. Concretely: an honest watcher's
  1701	    ///             `queueGuardianSlash(id, {A,B,C}, proof)` sits in the mempool; a
  1702	    ///             colluding or compromised owner front-runs it with this call for the
  1703	    ///             price of one transaction, and the watcher reverts with
  1704	    ///             `FraudProofVerifierNotSet`. Repeating it costs the owner one more
  1705	    ///             transaction each time. On 4.8.0 the same owner had to take
  1706	    ///             `propose(0)` + 4 PUBLICLY VISIBLE days, during which watchers could
  1707	    ///             still open cases that round-4's frozen verdict then protected.
  1708	    ///           • This power is accepted deliberately: a compromised verifier can slash
  1709	    ///             the configured fraction of each accused guardian's lock inside ONE block, so a five-day
  1710	    ///             remedy is not a remedy, and censoring future accusations is strictly
  1711	    ///             less damaging than that. It is a trade, not a free win.
  1712	    ///           • WHAT CARRIES THE RESIDUAL RISK: the owner's M-of-N configuration, and
  1713	    ///             nothing else. A TimelockController cannot cover this path — a
  1714	    ///             timelocked emergency stop is not an emergency stop — so for the DISARM
  1715	    ///             path the Safe-compatible M-of-N owner is the ONLY governance defence,
  1716	    ///             not defence in depth.
  1717	    ///             The in-contract 5-day delay still governs RE-ARMING: there is no
  1718	    ///             counterpart that sets a non-zero verifier, so coming back on-line
  1719	    ///             remains propose -> 5 days -> apply, and disarm is not a fast path to a
  1720	    ///             verifier of the owner's choosing.
  1721	    ///           • WHAT THE GATE ACTUALLY ENFORCES, STATED EXACTLY (CC-48 round-8
  1722	    ///             MEDIUM-2). `contracts/script/checks/GovernanceOwnerGate.sol` turns
  1723	    ///             "owner should be M-of-N" into a deploy/migration gate that fails closed
  1724	    ///             on an INTERFACE-AND-THRESHOLD basis: the owner holds code, is not an
  1725	    ///             EIP-7702 delegation designator, and answers `getThreshold() >= 2` plus
  1726	    ///             `getOwners()` as a canonical array of distinct non-zero owners of at
  1727	    ///             least that length. It does NOT prove the owner is a canonical Gnosis
  1728	    ///             Safe: a contract can implement both methods and return whatever it
  1729	    ///             likes while one key still controls execution. Downstream threat models
  1730	    ///             must consume the Safe-COMPATIBLE M-of-N property, never canonicity.
  1731	    ///             Earlier revisions of this comment asserted canonicity outright and
  1732	    ///             claimed the gate enforced it; both are RETRACTED as overstatements of
  1733	    ///             what any on-chain check in this repo can show.
  1734	    function emergencyDisarmFraudProofVerifier() external onlyOwner {
  1735	        address current = fraudProofVerifier;
  1736	        address pending = pendingFraudProofVerifier;
  1737	        uint64 pendingReadyAt = pendingFraudProofVerifierReadyAt;
  1738	        // Nothing to take away: refuse rather than emit a misleading "disarmed" trail.
  1739	        if (current == address(0) && pendingReadyAt == 0) revert VerifierAlreadyDisarmed();
  1740	
  1741	        if (current != address(0)) {
  1742	            fraudProofVerifier = address(0);
  1743	            emit FraudProofVerifierUpdated(current, address(0));
  1744	        }
  1745	        if (pendingReadyAt != 0) {
  1746	            delete pendingFraudProofVerifier;
  1747	            delete pendingFraudProofVerifierReadyAt;
  1748	            emit FraudProofVerifierRotationCancelled(pending);
  1749	        }
  1750	        emit FraudProofVerifierEmergencyDisarmed(current, pending);
  1751	    }
  1752	
  1753	    /// @notice Start a bounded ROLE_DVT unbonding notice. A guardian with a
  1754	    ///         notice remains eligible until readyAt, then stops signing for a
  1755	    ///         full fraud-challenge period before withdrawal opens.
  1756	    function requestGuardianExit() external {
  1757	        if (!REGISTRY.hasRole(keccak256("DVT"), msg.sender)) {
  1758	            revert SlotValidatorRoleRevoked(0, msg.sender);
  1759	        }
  1760	        if (pendingGuardianSlashCount[msg.sender] != 0) {
  1761	            revert GuardianExitBlockedBySlash(msg.sender, pendingGuardianSlashCount[msg.sender]);
  1762	        }
  1763	        if (guardianExitRequests[msg.sender].readyAt != 0) revert GuardianExitAlreadyRequested(msg.sender);
  1764	        uint64 cooldownUntil = guardianExitCooldownUntil[msg.sender];
  1765	        if (block.timestamp < uint256(cooldownUntil)) {
  1766	            revert GuardianExitCooldownActive(msg.sender, uint256(cooldownUntil));
  1767	        }
  1768	        _requireCommitteeSurvivesExit(msg.sender);
  1769	        uint64 readyAt = uint64(block.timestamp + GUARDIAN_EXIT_DELAY);
  1770	        uint64 expiresAt = uint64(uint256(readyAt) + GUARDIAN_FRAUD_CHALLENGE + GUARDIAN_EXIT_WINDOW);
  1771	        guardianExitRequests[msg.sender] = GuardianExitRequest(readyAt, expiresAt);
  1772	        emit GuardianExitRequested(msg.sender, readyAt, expiresAt);
  1773	    }
  1774	
  1775	    /// @notice Withdraw an exit notice and re-enter the signer set.
  1776	    /// @dev    CC-48 BLOCKER-1 / MEDIUM-5. Two changes over the original: an accused
  1777	    ///         guardian (pending case) may no longer cancel back into the signing set,
  1778	    ///         and every cancel arms a GUARDIAN_EXIT_COOLDOWN quiet period before a new
  1779	    ///         notice may be filed, so request/cancel cannot be cycled. This is also the
  1780	    ///         supported way to clear an EXPIRED notice: the record survives expiry (and
  1781	    ///         keeps excluding the slot), and cancelling is what puts the guardian back.
  1782	    function cancelGuardianExit() external {
  1783	        if (guardianExitRequests[msg.sender].readyAt == 0) revert GuardianExitNotRequested(msg.sender);
  1784	        uint256 pending = pendingGuardianSlashCount[msg.sender];
  1785	        if (pending != 0) revert GuardianExitBlockedBySlash(msg.sender, pending);
  1786	        delete guardianExitRequests[msg.sender];
  1787	        uint64 cooldownUntil = uint64(block.timestamp + GUARDIAN_EXIT_COOLDOWN);
  1788	        guardianExitCooldownUntil[msg.sender] = cooldownUntil;
  1789	        emit GuardianExitCancelled(msg.sender, cooldownUntil);
  1790	    }
  1791	
  1792	    /// @notice Whether losing one signer from this count breaks a configured quorum.
  1793	    function _isRequiredThreshold(uint256 count) internal view returns (bool) {
  1794	        if (count == minThreshold || count == defaultThreshold) return true;
  1795	        for (uint8 lvl = 0; lvl <= uint8(ISuperPaymasterSlash.SlashLevel.MAJOR);) {
  1796	            if (count == uint256(slashThresholds[lvl])) return true;
  1797	            unchecked {
  1798	                ++lvl;
  1799	            }
  1800	        }
  1801	        return false;
  1802	    }
  1803	
  1804	    /// @notice Preserve every currently reachable quorum when one guardian leaves.
  1805	    /// @dev Check both live role/stake-backed keys and the projected set without notices.
  1806	    ///      With one signer departing, before >= threshold && after < threshold
  1807	    ///      is exactly before == threshold. An unreachable higher threshold must
  1808	    ///      not hide a working lower one. The owner retains the explicit emergency
  1809	    ///      override in revokeBLSPublicKey/revokeAndSuspend and suspension.
  1810	    ///      At exactly reachable N-of-N, voluntary departure must wait for a
  1811	    ///      replacement or policy change. Protect minThreshold, defaultThreshold
  1812	    ///      and each severity floor; arbitrary higher caller thresholds are not policy.
  1813	    function _requireCommitteeSurvivesExit(address leaving) internal view {
  1814	        // A guardian with no active BLS key is not part of any signer mask, so its
  1815	        // departure cannot move the committee below quorum — nothing to check.
  1816	        if (!_blsKeys[leaving].isActive) return;
  1817	        bytes32 roleDvt = keccak256("DVT");
  1818	        // Maturity is the transition accepted when the notice was filed. A later
  1819	        // registration can make a higher quorum temporarily reachable until readyAt;
  1820	        // it does not revoke the notice or postpone its announced maturity.
  1821	        IGTokenStaking staking = IRegistryStakingAwareBLS(address(REGISTRY)).GTOKEN_STAKING();
  1822	        // Without staking, verification fails. The conservative role-only fallback
  1823	        // can still refuse an exit at an exact configured threshold.
  1824	        uint256 minStake = address(staking) == address(0) ? 0 : REGISTRY.getRoleConfig(roleDvt).minStake;
  1825	        uint256 remaining;
  1826	        uint256 remainingLive;
  1827	        bool leavingEligible;
  1828	        for (uint8 slot = 1; slot <= MAX_VALIDATORS;) {
  1829	            address v = validatorAtSlot[slot];
  1830	            unchecked {
  1831	                ++slot;
  1832	            }
  1833	            if (v == address(0)) continue;
  1834	            if (!_blsKeys[v].isActive || suspended[v]) continue;
  1835	            uint64 readyAt = guardianExitRequests[v].readyAt;
  1836	            if (readyAt != 0 && block.timestamp >= uint256(readyAt)) continue;
  1837	            if (!REGISTRY.hasRole(roleDvt, v)) continue;
  1838	            if (minStake != 0) {
  1839	                (uint128 lockedAmount,,,,) = staking.roleLocks(v, roleDvt);
  1840	                if (uint256(lockedAmount) < minStake) continue;
  1841	            }
  1842	            if (v == leaving) {
  1843	                leavingEligible = true;
  1844	                continue;
  1845	            }
  1846	            unchecked {
  1847	                ++remainingLive;
  1848	                if (readyAt == 0) ++remaining;
  1849	            }
  1850	        }
  1851	        if (!leavingEligible) return;
  1852	        uint256 before = remainingLive + 1;
  1853	        if (_isRequiredThreshold(before)) revert GuardianExitWouldBreakQuorum(remainingLive, before);
  1854	        // Removing an already-announced signer does not shrink the projected set.
  1855	        if (guardianExitRequests[leaving].readyAt != 0) return;
  1856	        before = remaining + 1;
  1857	        if (_isRequiredThreshold(before)) revert GuardianExitWouldBreakQuorum(remaining, before);
  1858	    }
  1859	
  1860	    /// @notice Registry-only atomic gate called by Registry.exitRole(ROLE_DVT).
  1861	    function consumeGuardianExit(address guardian) external {
  1862	        if (msg.sender != address(REGISTRY)) revert NotRegistry(msg.sender);
  1863	        GuardianExitRequest memory request = guardianExitRequests[guardian];
  1864	        if (request.readyAt == 0) revert GuardianExitNotRequested(guardian);
  1865	        if (pendingGuardianSlashCount[guardian] != 0) {
  1866	            revert GuardianExitBlockedBySlash(guardian, pendingGuardianSlashCount[guardian]);
  1867	        }
  1868	        uint256 withdrawAt = uint256(request.readyAt) + GUARDIAN_FRAUD_CHALLENGE;
  1869	        if (block.timestamp < withdrawAt) revert GuardianExitNotReady(guardian, withdrawAt);
  1870	        if (block.timestamp > request.expiresAt) revert GuardianExitRequestExpired(guardian, request.expiresAt);
  1871	        delete guardianExitRequests[guardian];
  1872	        emit GuardianExitConsumed(guardian);
  1873	    }
  1874	
  1875	    /// @notice Queue a verifier-approved guardian slash and freeze ROLE_DVT exits.
  1876	    /// @dev The full proof is checked before any freeze is installed, so arbitrary
  1877	    ///      callers cannot lock honest guardians. A bounded window
  1878	    ///      (`GUARDIAN_SLASH_CASE_WINDOW`, 5 days — it must strictly dominate a full
  1879	    ///      exit notice, see the constant's own comment) prevents an abandoned case
  1880	    ///      from becoming a permanent withdrawal denial.
  1881	    /// @dev `fraudProofId` is consumed for ever by this call, whatever the case's
  1882	    ///      eventual outcome, and the accused set is chosen by the CALLER. Verifiers
  1883	    ///      therefore MUST reject any set that is not exactly the one the evidence
  1884	    ///      commits to — otherwise a front-runner can open the case on a subset and
  1885	    ///      burn the id for everyone else. See `IFraudProofVerifier` above and
  1886	    ///      `FraudProofVerifierConformance.assertSetBound`.
  1887	    function queueGuardianSlash(uint256 fraudProofId, address[] calldata guiltyGuardians, bytes calldata fraudProof)
  1888	        external
  1889	        nonReentrant
  1890	    {
  1891	        address verifier = fraudProofVerifier;
  1892	        if (verifier == address(0)) revert FraudProofVerifierNotSet();
  1893	        // The single verify below is the ONLY time this address is ever asked about this
  1894	        // case, so it must be a contract: a call to an EOA succeeds with empty returndata,
  1895	        // and nothing about that outcome should be allowed near a verdict that is then
  1896	        // frozen for the life of the case.
  1897	        if (verifier.code.length == 0) revert VerifierNotContract(verifier);
  1898	        // CC-48 round-7 LOW-3: and it must not be an EIP-7702 delegation designator. See
  1880	    ///      from becoming a permanent withdrawal denial.
  1881	    /// @dev `fraudProofId` is consumed for ever by this call, whatever the case's
  1882	    ///      eventual outcome, and the accused set is chosen by the CALLER. Verifiers
  1883	    ///      therefore MUST reject any set that is not exactly the one the evidence
  1884	    ///      commits to — otherwise a front-runner can open the case on a subset and
  1885	    ///      burn the id for everyone else. See `IFraudProofVerifier` above and
  1886	    ///      `FraudProofVerifierConformance.assertSetBound`.
  1887	    function queueGuardianSlash(uint256 fraudProofId, address[] calldata guiltyGuardians, bytes calldata fraudProof)
  1888	        external
  1889	        nonReentrant
  1890	    {
  1891	        address verifier = fraudProofVerifier;
  1892	        if (verifier == address(0)) revert FraudProofVerifierNotSet();
  1893	        // The single verify below is the ONLY time this address is ever asked about this
  1894	        // case, so it must be a contract: a call to an EOA succeeds with empty returndata,
  1895	        // and nothing about that outcome should be allowed near a verdict that is then
  1896	        // frozen for the life of the case.
  1897	        if (verifier.code.length == 0) revert VerifierNotContract(verifier);
  1898	        // CC-48 round-7 LOW-3: and it must not be an EIP-7702 delegation designator. See
  1899	        // `VerifierIsDelegatedEoa` and `_isDelegatedEoa`.
  1900	        if (_isDelegatedEoa(verifier)) revert VerifierIsDelegatedEoa(verifier);
  1901	        if (fraudProofId == 0) revert InvalidProposalId();
  1902	        GuardianSlashCase storage slashCase = guardianSlashCases[fraudProofId];
  1903	        if (slashCase.status != 0) revert GuardianSlashCaseAlreadyOpened(fraudProofId);
  1904	        bytes32 guardiansHash = _validateGuardianSet(guiltyGuardians);
  1905	        if (!IFraudProofVerifier(verifier)
  1906	                .verify(fraudProofDigest(fraudProofId, guiltyGuardians), fraudProofId, guiltyGuardians, fraudProof)) {
  1907	            revert InvalidFraudProof(fraudProofId);
  1908	        }
  1909	
  1910	        uint64 deadline = uint64(block.timestamp + GUARDIAN_SLASH_CASE_WINDOW);
  1911	        // CC-48 round-4 HIGH: freeze the VERDICT here — the (guardian set, proof) pair the
  1912	        // verifier just approved. From this line on the case's fate depends only on state
  1913	        // in this contract, so nothing that happens to the verifier afterwards (rotation,
  1914	        // proxy implementation swap at the same address and codehash, selfdestruct, an
  1915	        // outage) can re-judge it. The verifier address is kept purely for attribution.
  1916	        bytes32 proofHash = keccak256(fraudProof);
  1917	        slashCase.guardiansHash = guardiansHash;
  1918	        slashCase.fraudProofHash = proofHash;
  1919	        slashCase.deadline = deadline;
  1920	        slashCase.status = 1;
  1921	        slashCase.slashBps = guardianSlashBps;
  1922	        slashCase.guardianCount = uint16(guiltyGuardians.length);
  1923	        slashCase.verifier = verifier;
  1924	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < guiltyGuardians.length;) {
  1925	            pendingGuardianSlashCount[guiltyGuardians[i]] += 1;
  1926	            unchecked {
  1927	                ++i;
  1928	            }
  1929	        }
  1930	        emit GuardianSlashQueued(fraudProofId, guardiansHash, deadline, guiltyGuardians);
  1931	        emit GuardianSlashJudgmentFrozen(fraudProofId, verifier, proofHash, guardiansHash);
  1932	    }
  1933	
  1934	    /// @notice Release an unexecuted case after its bounded pending window.
  1935	    function expireGuardianSlashCase(uint256 fraudProofId, address[] calldata guiltyGuardians) external nonReentrant {
  1936	        GuardianSlashCase storage slashCase = guardianSlashCases[fraudProofId];
  1937	        if (slashCase.status != 1) revert GuardianSlashCaseNotPending(fraudProofId);
  1938	        if (block.timestamp <= slashCase.deadline) {
  1939	            revert GuardianSlashCaseNotExpired(fraudProofId, slashCase.deadline);
  1940	        }
  1941	        if (_validateGuardianSet(guiltyGuardians) != slashCase.guardiansHash) {
  1942	            revert GuardianSetMismatch(fraudProofId);
  1943	        }
  1944	        slashCase.status = 3;
  1945	        // CC-48 round-5 LOW-4, decoder note: `resolvedCount` is deliberately NOT advanced
  1946	        // here. It counts guardians settled by EXECUTION (slashed or provably nothing to
  1947	        // take); expiry releases the stragglers without adjudicating them, and conflating
  1948	        // the two would make `status == 2` (fully executed) and "expired after a partial
  1949	        // execution" indistinguishable after the fact. Decoders must therefore read
  1950	        // `status == 3 && resolvedCount < guardianCount` as "expired with
  1951	        // `guardianCount - resolvedCount` accused released unjudged", which is exactly
  1952	        // what the `GuardianSlashed` / `GuardianSlashSkipped` event trail shows. This is
  1953	        // documented for SDK consumers in docs/security/CC48-round5-changes.md.
  1954	        // CC-48 HIGH-2: guardians already released by a successful (or no-op) partial
  1955	        // execution must not be decremented twice — expiry only frees the stragglers.
  1956	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < guiltyGuardians.length;) {
  1957	            address guardian = guiltyGuardians[i];
  1958	            if (!guardianCaseResolved[fraudProofId][guardian]) {
  1959	                guardianCaseResolved[fraudProofId][guardian] = true;
  1960	                pendingGuardianSlashCount[guardian] -= 1;
  1961	            }
  1962	            unchecked {
  1963	                ++i;
  1964	            }
  1965	        }
  1966	        emit GuardianSlashCaseExpired(fraudProofId);
  1967	    }
  1968	
  1969	    /// @notice Slash the FULL ROLE_DVT stake of guardians proven (by the external
  1970	    ///         fraud-proof verifier) to have co-signed a fraudulent proposal. This
  1971	    ///         is the thin SP execution entry for the paper's ρ·S_op collusion
  1972	    ///         deterrent (Protocol B stage 1). It complements _executeSlash, which
  1973	    ///         targets the operator's aPNTs — a DIFFERENT asset (guardian ROLE_DVT
  1974	    ///         stake ≠ operator aPNTs, even if the addresses coincide).
  1975	    ///
  1976	    /// @dev    - Guardians are addressed DIRECTLY, not via slot: validatorAtSlot is
  1977	    ///           reassignable (revokeBLSPublicKey frees a slot for reuse), so a slot
  1978	    ///           captured at fraud time could later resolve to an innocent validator
  1979	    ///           — slashing by slot would hit the wrong address. The verifier binds
  1980	    ///           the proof to stable addresses; the slash reads the accused's own
  1981	    ///           ROLE_DVT lock. This blocks the revoke-KEY/slot variant (a slashed
  1982	    ///           address's lock is independent of whether it still holds a slot).
  1983	    ///           Registry.exitRole(ROLE_DVT) calls consumeGuardianExit. A guardian
  1984	    ///           must first publish a bounded exit notice, is excluded from BLS signer
  1985	    ///           masks from readyAt and can withdraw only after GUARDIAN_FRAUD_CHALLENGE;
  1986	    ///           any queued slash case freezes consumption.
  1987	    ///           GuardianSlashSkipped remains for legacy/direct staking drift, but
  1988	    ///           the coordinated Registry path cannot release a queued guardian.
  1989	    ///         - Permissionless CALL, gated by the verdict frozen at queue time: fraud
  1990	    ///           validity — not caller identity — authorizes the slash. This deliberately
  1991	    ///           bypasses the accused DVT quorum, which is the collusion set and would
  1992	    ///           never slash itself (the circular-dependency escape).
  1993	    ///         - Penalty uses the queue-time slashBps snapshot (default 30%) applied
  1994	    ///           to the lock at execution. Losing eligibility is not guaranteed:
  1995	    ///           over-staked guardians may still meet minStake after the penalty.
  1996	    ///         - fail-closed: no case can exist until a verifier is wired, because
  1997	    ///           `queueGuardianSlash` — the only way to open one — reverts without it.
  1998	    /// @param  fraudProofId    Unique id of the fraud proof (own id-space; replay-guarded).
  1999	    /// @param  guiltyGuardians Addresses proven to have colluded (bound by the verifier at
  2000	    ///                         queue time); must hash to the case's `guardiansHash`.
  2001	    /// @param  fraudProof      The SAME proof bytes the verifier approved at queue time;
  2002	    ///                         checked against `fraudProofHash`, not re-interpreted here.
  2003	    function executeGuardianSlash(uint256 fraudProofId, address[] calldata guiltyGuardians, bytes calldata fraudProof)
  2004	        external
  2005	        nonReentrant
  2006	    {
  2007	        // All fail-closed shape checks up front (fail-fast, before any external call).
  2008	        // CC-48 round-5 LOW-4, kept deliberately: these two bounds are re-checked inside
  2009	        // `_validateGuardianSet` further down, but that call happens only AFTER the case
  2010	        // lookup and the deadline check. Running them here keeps a malformed call cheap
  2011	        // and, more importantly, keeps the failure mode stable if the order of the
  2012	        // checks below is ever rearranged. They are pure `calldatasize`-derived
  2013	        // comparisons — no state, no external call — so the duplication costs a few gas
  2014	        // on the revert path only. It is redundancy, not dead code: removing them is
  2015	        // safe today and silently unsafe after the next reordering.
  2016	        if (guiltyGuardians.length == 0) revert EmptyGuiltyGuardians();
  2017	        if (guiltyGuardians.length > MAX_VALIDATORS) revert InvalidParameter("guiltyGuardians");
  2018	        GuardianSlashCase storage slashCase = guardianSlashCases[fraudProofId];
  2019	        if (slashCase.status != 1) revert GuardianSlashCaseNotPending(fraudProofId);
  2020	        if (block.timestamp > slashCase.deadline) {
  2021	            revert GuardianSlashCaseExpiredError(fraudProofId, slashCase.deadline);
  2022	        }
  2023	        // CC-48 round-4 HIGH: the case is decided by the verdict frozen at queue time, and
  2024	        // the ONLY thing execution re-checks is that the caller re-presents exactly the
  2025	        // (guardian set, proof) pair that verdict covers. There is no external verifier
  2026	        // call on this path at all.
  2027	        //
  2028	        // Round-3 re-verified against the address pinned at queue time. That closed the
  2029	        // rotation variant but not the general one: an upgradeable verifier (UUPS /
  2030	        // Transparent / Beacon) survives an implementation swap with the SAME address and
  2031	        // the SAME extcodehash, so a queued case could still be flipped to `false` after
  2032	        // the fact and starved to expiry. `code.length`, an address snapshot and a plain
  2033	        // extcodehash all fail to witness "the semantics behind this address did not
  2034	        // change", so none of them can carry the property. Freezing the verdict removes
  2035	        // the dependency instead of trying to constrain it — and as a bonus a verifier
  2036	        // that selfdestructs, reverts, or is simply down can no longer strand a case.
  2037	        if (_validateGuardianSet(guiltyGuardians) != slashCase.guardiansHash) {
  2038	            revert GuardianSetMismatch(fraudProofId);
  2039	        }
  2040	        bytes32 providedProofHash = keccak256(fraudProof);
  2041	        if (providedProofHash != slashCase.fraudProofHash) {
  2042	            revert FraudProofMismatch(fraudProofId, slashCase.fraudProofHash, providedProofHash);
  2043	        }
  2044	        IGTokenStaking staking = IRegistryStakingAwareBLS(address(REGISTRY)).GTOKEN_STAKING();
  2045	        if (address(staking) == address(0)) revert StakingNotConfigured();
  2046	
  2047	        // Consumption is tracked per guardian below, NOT by a single global id — so an
  2048	        // already-exited co-signer can never burn the proof for the others.
  2049	        bytes32 roleDvt = keccak256("DVT");
  2050	
  2051	        // CC-48 HIGH-2: advance guardian-by-guardian. Pre-fix this loop marked the
  2052	        // whole case executed up front and let any single `slashByDVT` revert take the
  2053	        // entire transaction down; if that condition (staking paused, authorization
  2054	        // rotated, an odd lock state) simply outlasted the deadline, expiry then
  2055	        // released EVERY accused guardian at once. Now each guardian is settled on its
  2056	        // own: successes are banked, failures leave that guardian frozen and the case
  2057	        // pending, and the caller can retry until the deadline.
  2058	        uint256 released;
  2059	        for (uint256 i = 0; i < guiltyGuardians.length;) {
  2060	            address guardian = guiltyGuardians[i];
  2061	            unchecked {
  2062	                ++i;
  2063	            }
  2064	            if (guardianCaseResolved[fraudProofId][guardian]) continue;
  2065	
  2066	            (uint128 amount,,,,) = staking.roleLocks(guardian, roleDvt);
  2067	            if (amount == 0) {
  2068	                // Exited / already-ejected: nothing left to take. Settle it so the
  2069	                // still-staked colluders are not held hostage by this entry.
  2070	                guardianCaseResolved[fraudProofId][guardian] = true;
  2071	                pendingGuardianSlashCount[guardian] -= 1;
  2072	                unchecked {
  2073	                    ++released;
  2074	                }
  2075	                emit GuardianSlashSkipped(fraudProofId, guardian);
  2076	                continue;
  2077	            }
  2078	
  2079	            // Effects deliberately AFTER the call here: `nonReentrant` already blocks
  2080	            // re-entry into this contract's guarded entry points, and leaving
  2081	            // `pendingGuardianSlashCount` untouched for the duration of the external
  2082	            // call keeps Registry.exitRole -> consumeGuardianExit closed against a
  2083	            // guardian that tries to walk out from inside its own slash.
  2084	            // Fraction of what is left, not all of it. Rounding down can reach zero on a
  2085	            // dust-sized lock; taking the dust in full is the conservative branch, because
  2086	            // a zero penalty would still mark the case resolved and let a colluder settle
  2087	            // for nothing.
  2088	            // The queue-time snapshot, never the live value: see GuardianSlashCase.slashBps.
  2089	            uint16 bps = slashCase.slashBps;
  2090	            // Only reachable for a case queued before this field existed, impossible on
  2091	            // a fresh deployment. Fail loudly rather than fall back to the live value,
  2092	            // which restores the hole this snapshot closes, or to a zero penalty, which
  2093	            // the line below would then widen to the whole lock.
  2094	            if (bps == 0) revert InvalidParameter("guardianSlashCaseBps");
  2095	            uint256 penalty = (uint256(amount) * bps) / _BPS_DENOMINATOR;
  2096	            if (penalty == 0) penalty = uint256(amount);
  2097	
  2098	            try IGTokenStakingSlash(address(staking)).slashByDVT(guardian, roleDvt, penalty, "DVT collusion") {
  2099	                guardianSlashed[fraudProofId][guardian] = true;
  2100	                guardianCaseResolved[fraudProofId][guardian] = true;
  2101	                pendingGuardianSlashCount[guardian] -= 1;
  2102	                unchecked {
  2103	                    ++released;
  2104	                }
  2105	                emit GuardianSlashed(fraudProofId, guardian, penalty);
  2106	            } catch {
  2107	                // Stays unresolved and stays frozen — retryable until the deadline.
  2108	                emit GuardianSlashFailed(fraudProofId, guardian);
  2109	            }
  2110	        }
  2111	
  2112	        if (released != 0) {
  2113	            uint16 resolved = slashCase.resolvedCount + uint16(released);
  2114	            slashCase.resolvedCount = resolved;
  2115	            if (resolved == slashCase.guardianCount) {
  2116	                slashCase.status = 2;
  2117	                emit GuardianSlashCaseResolved(fraudProofId);
  2118	            }
  2119	        }
  2120	    }
  2121	
  2122	    /// @dev CC-48 round-7 LOW-3. True iff `account` carries an EIP-7702 delegation
  2123	    ///      designator: exactly 23 bytes of code beginning `0xef 0x01 0x00`. That encoding
  2124	    ///      is fixed by EIP-7702 and no deployable contract can produce it — `0xef` is a
  2125	    ///      reserved initial byte (EIP-3541), so a CREATE/CREATE2 runtime can never start
  2126	    ///      with it. There is therefore no false positive to trade against.
  2127	    ///
  2128	    ///      Only the first three bytes are copied, so this costs one `EXTCODESIZE` plus one
  2129	    ///      3-word `EXTCODECOPY` regardless of how large the target is.
  2130	    function _isDelegatedEoa(address account) private view returns (bool) {
  2131	        bytes3 prefix;
  2132	        uint256 size;
  2133	        assembly {
  2134	            size := extcodesize(account)
  2135	            if eq(size, 23) {
  2136	                // Scratch space (0x00-0x3f) is caller-free. Zero it first: EXTCODECOPY
  2137	                // writes only the 3 bytes asked for, and a dirty tail in the same word
  2138	                // would survive into the bytes3.
  2139	                mstore(0x00, 0)
  2140	                extcodecopy(account, 0x00, 0, 3)
  2141	                prefix := mload(0x00)
  2142	            }
  2143	        }
  2144	        return size == 23 && prefix == 0xef0100;
  2145	    }

   282	        address old = address(MYSBT);

   301	    function _enforceMinStake(uint256 stakeAmount, uint256 minStake) internal pure returns (uint256) {
   302	        if (minStake == 0) return 0;
   303	        if (stakeAmount == 0) stakeAmount = minStake;
   304	        if (stakeAmount < minStake) revert InsufficientStake(stakeAmount, minStake);
   305	        return stakeAmount;
   306	    }

   308	    function _requireCommunityForPaymaster(bytes32 roleId, address user) internal view {
   309	        if (roleId == ROLE_PAYMASTER_SUPER || roleId == ROLE_PAYMASTER_AOA) {
   310	             if (!hasRole[ROLE_COMMUNITY][user]) revert RoleNotGranted(ROLE_COMMUNITY, user);
   330	        if (msg.sender != user) revert Unauthorized();
   331	        if (roleData.length > 2048) revert InvalidParam();
   332	
   333	        RoleConfig memory config = roleConfigs[roleId];
   334	        if (!config.isActive) revert RoleNotConfigured(roleId, config.isActive);
   335	
   336	        bool alreadyHasRole = hasRole[roleId][user];
   337	        if (alreadyHasRole && roleId != ROLE_ENDUSER) revert RoleAlreadyGranted(roleId, user);
   338	
   339	        _requireCommunityForPaymaster(roleId, user);
   340	
   341	        (uint256 stakeAmount, bytes memory sbtData) = _validateAndProcessRole(roleId, user, roleData);
   342	        stakeAmount = _enforceMinStake(stakeAmount, config.minStake);
   343	
   344	        if (!alreadyHasRole) {
   345	            _firstTimeRegister(roleId, user, roleData, stakeAmount, config.ticketPrice, user);
   346	        } else {
   347	            if (stakeAmount > roleStakes[roleId][user]) {
   348	                GTOKEN_STAKING.topUpStake(user, roleId, stakeAmount - roleStakes[roleId][user], user);
   349	                roleStakes[roleId][user] = stakeAmount;
   350	            }
   351	            roleMetadata[roleId][user] = roleData;
   352	        }
   353	
   354	        (uint256 sbtTokenId, ) = MYSBT.mintForRole(user, roleId, sbtData);
   355	        roleSBTTokenIds[roleId][user] = sbtTokenId;
   356	        emit RoleRegistered(roleId, user, alreadyHasRole ? 0 : config.ticketPrice, block.timestamp);
   357	    }
   358	
   359	    function exitRole(bytes32 roleId) external nonReentrant {
   360	        if (!hasRole[roleId][msg.sender]) revert RoleNotGranted(roleId, msg.sender);
   361	        if (roleId == ROLE_DVT) {
   362	            if (blsAggregator == address(0)) revert BLSNotConfigured();
   363	            IGuardianExitGate(blsAggregator).consumeGuardianExit(msg.sender);
   364	        }
   365	
   366	        // M-6: gate real fund release on Staking's source-of-truth, NOT the local
   367	        // `roleStakes` cache. The cache is synced best-effort (try/catch in
   368	        // GTokenStaking._syncRegistry); if a sync ever failed it could be stale,
   369	        // and gating on it would either strand a real lock (cache 0, lock > 0) or
   370	        // brick exit via NoLockFound (cache > 0, lock already cleared).
   371	        bool hasStake = GTOKEN_STAKING.getLockedStake(msg.sender, roleId) > 0;
   372	
   373	        hasRole[roleId][msg.sender] = false;
   374	        _removeFromRoleMembers(roleId, msg.sender);
   375	        if (userRoleCount[msg.sender] > 0) {
   376	            userRoleCount[msg.sender]--;
   377	        }
   378	        _removeFromUserRoles(msg.sender, roleId);
   379	
   380	        if (roleId == ROLE_ENDUSER && userRoleCount[msg.sender] == 0) {
   880	        // Checked on EVERY batch, not only the finalizing one: a partial count that already
   881	        // exceeds the ceiling can only get worse, and leaving an over-ceiling number in the
   882	        // slot between batches is a state no later call would re-examine.
   883	        uint256 cap = maxTotalCreditExposure;
   884	        if (exposure > cap) revert TotalCreditExposureExceeded(exposure, cap);
   885	        if (finalize) {
   886	            if (counted != expectedPopulationTotal) revert CreditPopulationCountMismatch();
   887	            creditPopulationSeededAt = block.timestamp;
   888	        }
   889	        emit CreditExposureResynced(exposure);
   890	    }

# Daybreak-blue defensive audit

Scope: main commit `053117cc8ca31eb69ae1f62755a1d7790e71190f`, principally `BLSAggregator-4.14.1` and `DVTValidator-0.9.0`, including Registry, SuperPaymaster, GTokenStaking, and ReputationSystem call-outs.

Disclosure note: DVTValidator “rule A” was authored by Codex, the same model family as this reviewer. This review therefore independently re-traced the implementation but is not model-independent validation.

No files were modified, no network/RPC/secrets were accessed, and no Forge formatting or deployment command was run. `scripts/check_generic_selector_coverage.py` passed: 111 functions enumerated, 6 blocked, 20 allowed-with-reasons, 13 deployment grants mapped.

## NEW finding

### M-01 — A completed guardian exit does not retire its BLS seat or DVT admission

Severity: Medium

Locations:

- [BLSAggregator.sol:1861](/Users/jason/Dev/aastar/sp-wt/rc8-bls/contracts/src/modules/monitoring/BLSAggregator.sol:1861)
- [BLSAggregator.sol:1298](/Users/jason/Dev/aastar/sp-wt/rc8-bls/contracts/src/modules/monitoring/BLSAggregator.sol:1298)
- [DVTValidator.sol:296](/Users/jason/Dev/aastar/sp-wt/rc8-bls/contracts/src/modules/monitoring/DVTValidator.sol:296)
- [Registry.sol:359](/Users/jason/Dev/aastar/sp-wt/rc8-bls/contracts/src/core/Registry.sol:359)

Concrete precondition and call sequence:

1. A validator has `ROLE_DVT`, a live BLS slot, and `DVTValidator.isValidator[validator] == true`.
2. It calls `requestGuardianExit()`, waits through `readyAt + GUARDIAN_FRAUD_CHALLENGE`, then calls `Registry.exitRole(ROLE_DVT)`.
3. `consumeGuardianExit()` only deletes the exit notice. It neither revokes/suspends the BLS key nor clears DVT local admission.
4. Registry removes the role and unlocks the stake, temporarily making the slot ineligible.
5. The same address calls permissionless `Registry.registerRole(ROLE_DVT, ...)` again and supplies the required stake.
6. `_reconstructPkAgg()` now sees the old key as active, unsuspended, role-bearing, and adequately staked. `_requireActiveValidator()` also accepts the stale local admission.
7. The address can immediately propose and sign again without a fresh PoP, `addValidator`, owner approval, or enabling `permissionlessBLSRegistration`.

Impact:

A validator that has completed an exit can silently restore both proposer and signer powers. This bypasses the owner-curated key-registration switch and any intended post-exit re-admission process. It also leaves exited seats occupying scarce slots until governance explicitly revokes them.

This does not bypass an explicit `setSuspended`/`revokeAndSuspend`; a suspended validator remains excluded. The issue is that ordinary successful exit is not itself a signer-set removal.

Reachable in 5.5.0: Yes. Slash categories and `fraudProofVerifier` are irrelevant. It requires a previously admitted guardian, successful completion of the exit procedure, and enough GToken to register/stake for `ROLE_DVT` again.

Minimal fix:

On successful `consumeGuardianExit`, retire the active BLS registration while retaining the key-owner binding. To make re-admission explicitly owner-controlled, also suspend the guardian. If permissionless re-entry is desired instead, at minimum:

- revoke the active slot on consumed exit;
- require a fresh registration and PoP before the slot can vote again; and
- make `_requireActiveValidator` require an active BLS key or clear `DVTValidator.isValidator` through a coordinated callback.

Add a regression covering exit → role reacquisition and proving that neither proposal creation nor signature reconstruction succeeds before the chosen re-admission procedure.

## Generic `executeProposal` privilege inventory

The aggregator is not owner, role owner, operator, or DVT-role holder in the asserted 5.5.0 configuration. It is:

- SuperPaymaster’s configured BLS identity;
- Registry’s configured BLS identity and a reputation source;
- DVTValidator’s configured aggregator;
- indirectly accepted by ReputationSystem because Registry identifies it as a reputation source;
- not an authorized GTokenStaking slasher in 5.5.0.

| Contract / identity | Privileged function | Generic result |
|---|---|---|
| SuperPaymaster / `BLS_AGGREGATOR` | `queueSlash(address)` | Blocked by selector denylist |
| SuperPaymaster / `BLS_AGGREGATOR` | `executeSlashWithBLS(address,uint8,bytes)` | Blocked |
| SuperPaymaster / BLS or owner | `updatePriceDVT(int256,uint256,bytes,uint8)` | Allowed; freshness, monotonic timestamp, absolute bounds, and BLS ±20% cache band remain |
| Registry / reputation source | `batchUpdateGlobalReputation(...)` | Blocked |
| Registry / `blsAggregator` | `markProposalExecuted(uint256)` | Blocked |
| Registry / `blsAggregator` | `updateOperatorBlacklist(...)` | Allowed; performs its own default-threshold proof and nonce verification |
| GTokenStaking / authorized slasher | `slash(address,uint256,string)` | Blocked even if a future configuration grants the role |
| GTokenStaking / authorized slasher | `slashByDVT(address,bytes32,uint256,string)` | Blocked |
| DVTValidator / `BLS_AGGREGATOR` | `markProposalExecuted(uint256)` | Blocked |
| DVTValidator / authorized executor | `executeWithProof(...)` | Not denylisted, but re-entry into `verifyAndExecute` fails on `nonReentrant` |
| DVTValidator | `createProposal` / `queueSlashWithProof` | Aggregator lacks DVT role, stake, and local admission |
| DVTValidator | `registerBLSPublicKey` | Aggregator would have to register itself and satisfy permissionless role/stake/PoP checks; unreachable in asserted configuration |
| ReputationSystem / reputation source | `setCommunityReputation(...)` | Allowed; known unused-state residual |
| Arbitrary ERC-20 | `transfer` / `approve` | Allowed if the aggregator holds assets; known zero-balance deployment invariant |

Owner-only functions in these contracts remain unreachable because the aggregator is not their owner. Registry role-owner functions likewise remain unreachable because it owns no role. This matches the classification table.

The coverage script is appropriately treated as a source-text regression checker, not a complete authorization proof: aliases, inherited gates, new external targets, or fallback authorization could escape it.

## Reputation and queue ingress results

### DVT → `verifyAndExecute`

Confirmed controls:

- Current-DVT calls require `proposalId >= proposalFloor`.
- Reputation uses `defaultThreshold`.
- Slash uses the configured per-level threshold.
- Proposal shape comes from stored DVT fields.
- Pure reputation requires stored `(operator=0, level=0, evidenceHash=0)`, non-empty `repUsers`, and empty evidence.
- Slash requires a non-reputation stored shape, configured evidence category, accepted checker result, and exact stored evidence hash.
- Current signer role, stake, suspension, key activity, and matured exit status are rechecked at execution.

No rule-A bypass found on this ingress.

### `ReputationSystem.syncToRegistry` → Registry

Known/disclosed confirmation:

- [ReputationSystem.sol:194](/Users/jason/Dev/aastar/sp-wt/rc8-bls/contracts/src/modules/reputation/ReputationSystem.sol:194) derives a full-width hash proposal ID and calls Registry directly.
- Registry applies `defaultThreshold`, replay protection, per-user epoch progression, score clamping, and credit exposure caps.
- It has no `proposalFloor`; therefore unused pre-rotation proofs remain executable through this authorized source.

Reachable in 5.5.0: Yes.

Minimal containment if this residual is no longer accepted: add an aggregator-generation/floor component to the Registry proof domain or route all reputation updates through the current DVT executor.

### Owner-direct `verifyAndExecute`

Known/disclosed confirmation:

- Owner calls bypass `proposalFloor`.
- They still require the appropriate BLS threshold, current signer eligibility, and unused raw proposal ID.
- Slash shape and `evidenceHash` are caller-supplied rather than read from DVT storage; no DVT category checker runs.
- Reputation shape still enforces zero operator/level through the combined-shape guard.

Reachable in 5.5.0: Reputation is reachable; operator slash remains ineffective without a prior queue and, in disclosure-only configuration, the separate guardian-stake mechanism is disabled.

Minimal containment: if owner-mediated stale authorization or evidence bypass is no longer accepted, remove the owner entry or require owner calls to reference a stored DVT proposal at or above the floor.

### Queue paths

`DVTValidator.queueSlashWithProof`:

- Requires an active, eligible, unsuspended DVT validator.
- Requires a configured category and accepted evidence checker.
- Uses the per-severity slash threshold.
- Has no proposal ID/floor by design.
- The subsequent SuperPaymaster call enforces its BLS queue cooldown.

`BLSAggregator.queueSlashWithConsensus` owner path:

- Requires the same threshold and proof.
- Bypasses the DVT evidence-category check and proposal floor.
- Still encounters SuperPaymaster’s BLS cooldown because SuperPaymaster sees the aggregator as caller.

In 5.5.0, `categoryCount()==0` makes the DVT queue path unreachable. The owner path can consume an existing valid queue proof, but it moves no funds and is a disclosed reversible pre-flag. No new queue cooldown or threshold bypass was found.

## Signer-set integrity confirmations

- Registration is forwarded only by the current DVT, and both owner and permissionless paths require address/domain/key-bound PoP.
- Keys are validated for non-identity, curve membership, and subgroup membership.
- `blsKeyOwner` prevents the same public key from multiplying voting weight across addresses.
- Permissionless registration requires the caller to equal the validator plus live role/stake eligibility; suspension cannot be cleared through registration.
- Revocation clears the slot but retains the key-owner binding. `releaseKeyBinding` is owner-only and refuses a binding still used by an active registration.
- Verification rejects empty/out-of-range masks and rechecks active key, suspension, role, stake, and matured exit status for every selected slot.
- All signed paths bind chain ID, aggregator address, Registry address, and a distinct path tag. Slash/reputation IDs and generic/queue typed keys occupy separated replay namespaces.
- Signer commitments include the verified message, mask, sorted signer addresses, domain, typed key, and preceding block hash, so later slot reuse cannot rewrite attribution.
- Rotation does not cryptographically change the domain because the DVT address is not included. The documented `proposalFloor` invalidates current-DVT slash/reputation execution, while generic, direct Registry, owner-direct, and unused queue proofs retain their disclosed rotation behavior.

## Known items explicitly not re-reported

I did not count as new findings:

- disclosure-only guardian slashing (`categoryCount()==0`, `fraudProofVerifier==0`, aggregator not an authorized slasher);
- the residuals already listed in `docs/security/rc8-daybreak-fixes.md`;
- deployer-held owner, treasury, role-owner, `daoMultisig`, `slashPolicyAdmin`, zero guardian, and legacy uncapped aPNTs privileges being handled by the pending manifest/deploy work.

NEW findings — Critical: 0, High: 0, Medium: 1, Low: 0, Info: 0.
